# Traceability — OPEN items only

> **This is the file the routine reads every run.** It contains only rows whose status is `open`, `in-progress`,
> `blocked` or `deferred-with-reason`. Closed rows are archived under `traceability/CLOSED-*.md` and are **never**
> re-read during selection — that re-reading is what made every run slower than the last.
>
> Partitioned 2026-08-08 by the throughput change (S1). Nothing was deleted — every row lives in exactly one file.

**When you close a finding:** move its row out of this file and append it to the matching `CLOSED-<layer>.md`,
with its evidence. Never delete a row.


## 1. Layer 0 — Trust and production foundation

| PF-56 observability/SLO/restore unproven | V3-E02 | **S-E02-13** *(metrics)*, **S-E02-14** *(traces)*, **S-E02-15** *(web)*, **S-E02-17** *(queue third)* | `in-progress` | `apps/api/src/shared/quality/observability-gate.spec.ts` — **33/33**; `apps/api/src/modules/metrics/metrics.spec.ts` — **13/13**; `apps/worker/src/shared/observability/metrics-server.spec.ts` — **6/6**, on a **real socket** | **The finding said "optional rather than proven active". Measured, the profile could not start at all — and that measurement is the run's main result.** `infra/docker-compose.yml` has declared an `obs` profile (Prometheus/Grafana/Loki) since it was written; **all three** of its bind-mount sources (`./grafana/prometheus.yml`, `./grafana/dashboards`, `./grafana/provisioning`) **did not exist in the repository** — 3 of the 8 relative bind-mount sources in that file, and all 3 this profile. Docker creates a *directory* for a missing bind-mount source, so Prometheus would have received a directory where it expects its config file; and no application registered a single metric, so a working Prometheus would have scraped nothing. R-26's sixth instance in seven runs. **What landed:** a Prometheus registry and `GET /metrics` on the API (default process metrics + an HTTP latency histogram and request counter, the two an SLO actually needs), the same exposition on the worker's existing release-manifest socket, the three missing config files written for real (scrape config, provisioned datasource, provisioned 4-panel SLO dashboard), and `scripts/observability-check.js` as **stage 9** of `ci-gate.sh` and a step in `ci.yml`. **The cardinality rule is the security rule:** `/metrics` is unauthenticated by construction, so series are labelled by the **matched route template** and never by the resolved URL — a test drives the label function with a request carrying both and asserts the id does not reach the exposition (G-TENANT), and the gate fails if nginx ever publishes the path. **The check caught its own first defect:** merging compose files with `Object.assign` let `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s *partial* `api` override replace the base definition wholesale, and it reported "listening port cannot be determined" for both applications on its first run; it now merges the way compose merges. **Executed in both directions** — see the run log. **Not closed, and the residual is most of the finding.** *(Updated by run 16: the **trace third is now closed** — `S-E02-14` / `PF-78`. Both Nest applications emit redacted spans, proven by an executed request rather than by a configuration read. What follows is the rest.)* Queue depth / failure / DLQ are not exposed, and no official BullMQ instrumentation exists, so job processing is untraced as well as unmeasured; **no alert rule or SLO threshold is defined** — that is a product decision about what "good" means; `apps/web` is covered by neither surface (`PF-79`); and the restore third stays blocked on **D-01**. Above all, this does **not** start Prometheus and watch a scrape succeed — the profile is proven *coherent and complete*, not *ingesting* **Updated 2026-08-07 (run 24) — the queue third is now EXECUTED against a running container, and that is the first time any observability claim in this row has been proven that way.** `S-E02-17` registers `pilotage_queue_depth{queue,state}`, `pilotage_queue_jobs_total{queue,job,outcome}`, `pilotage_queue_job_duration_seconds{queue,job}` and `pilotage_queue_depth_collection_failures_total{queue}` on the worker registry, adds Grafana panels 5–8, and extends `scripts/observability-check.js` (ci-gate stage 9) so instrumented queues ≡ registered queues. Full `scripts/ci-gate.sh`: **`GATE: PASS`, exit 0, 15/15 stages** (R-23 — the verdict line, not a stage selection), with `test-ratchet` reporting **no drift** on api *and* worker (the `PF-80` check). **Step 2 measured something the rows above could not have known:** `pilotage_worker` was running an image built **2026-06-06**, two months older than `S-E02-10`/`13`/`14`/`15`, whose `dist/` contained neither `shared/release/` nor `shared/observability/` — so `127.0.0.1:4001` refused connections and **0** series existed in the *running* stack while every row above read green. That is not a code defect (`EXPECTED_GIT_SHA` is unset, which by `S-E02-10`’s deliberate design yields `unverified` and blocks nothing) but it is a fact about this routine’s evidence base, recorded as **`PF-111`**. The worker was therefore rebuilt and recreated, per Step −1. **What the rebuild then proved, by execution rather than by unit test:** the container boots healthy, `QueueDepthCollector` logs « Profondeur de file instrumentée pour 3 file(s) : exports, notifications-email, imports », and a real scrape returns **24 depth samples** — 3 queues × **8** BullMQ states, because the implementation measured the state list instead of trusting this story’s six; `prioritized` and `waiting-children` are real states a six-state list would have silently dropped. A probe job was then enqueued with `attempts: 1` and a payload carrying `tenantId: "probe-tenant"`, `exportJobId: "nonexistent-probe-id"` and `requestedBy: "routine-run-24"`; it failed in the processor, and the exposition moved to `pilotage_queue_jobs_total{queue="exports",job="<other>",outcome="failed_terminal"} 1`, `pilotage_queue_job_duration_seconds_count{...} 1`, and `pilotage_queue_depth{queue="exports",state="failed"}` **0 → 1**. **G-TENANT is therefore discharged against the live artefact, not only in a spec:** the job *name* was `probe-terminal-failure`, which is not allow-listed, and it rendered as the `<other>` sentinel; none of the three identifiers appears anywhere in the 12 742-byte exposition (`grep -c` = **0**). Probe removed afterwards; the stack was left **up and healthy**. **Not closed, and the residual is why the PR was NOT auto-merged.** The verify panel confirmed three defects *inside the mechanism this slice makes blocking*: **`PF-104`** — a job that fails by *stalling* emits `stalled`, never `failed`, so an OOM-killed or SIGKILLed worker under-reports precisely when the system is broken; **`PF-105`** — check 9’s rule 6 compares a constant against labels rendered *from* that constant, i.e. a guard that cannot fail; **`PF-106`** — no test executes the `@OnWorkerEvent` seam or `onModuleInit`, **re-scoped by this run’s evidence** from “the capability may be dead” to “the capability is live, proven once by hand, and nothing re-proves it”. Plus **`PF-107`**/**`PF-108`** (the outage signal is plotted on no panel; a shared-resource gauge is `sum`med across replicas, so `--scale worker=2` doubles every line), **`PF-109`** and **`PF-110`**. Alert rules and SLO thresholds remain **undefined** — a product decision, and reason enough for PF-56 to stay open regardless. And nothing was *ingested*: no Prometheus scrape, no Grafana render — the `obs` profile was not started this run **Updated 2026-08-08 (run 26) — it is ingested now, and that sentence is the one this row has carried since run 15.** Every observability claim above stopped at *emitted and coherent*, because starting the profile was believed to be forbidden; under Step −1 it is not, so `docker compose --env-file .env -f infra/docker-compose.yml --profile obs up -d` was run. **All four Prometheus scrape targets report `up`** — `pilotage-api` (`api:4000`), `pilotage-worker` (`worker:4001`), `pilotage-web` (`web:3001`) and `prometheus` itself — with no `lastError` on any of them, which is the first time in this programme that any of the three expositions has been read by something other than the check script that spawned it. Dashboard expressions were then resolved **against the real TSDB rather than against the JSON**: `max by (queue, state) (pilotage_queue_depth)` returned all 24 series, `sum by (queue) (rate(pilotage_queue_depth_collection_failures_total[5m]))` returned one series per queue, and `pilotage_queue_stalled_total` returned the sample the SIGKILL drill produced. **Grafana rendered too**: `GET /api/dashboards/uid/pilotage-slo` returns « Pilotage — SLO » with **nine** panels including the new *Jobs bloqués (stalled) par file*, and the provisioned datasource `pilotage-prometheus` resolves to `http://prometheus:9090`. **Still open, and the reason is unchanged:** alert rules and SLO thresholds are undefined, which is a product decision this routine does not take. **Newly honest limits:** nothing asserts a *panel renders data* (the Grafana API confirms provisioning, not a rendered graph), Loki ingests nothing application-side, and Jaeger was started but no span arrival was asserted — the tracing evidence remains *emitted*, not *ingested* | VAL-09, G-TENANT |
| PF-77 routine's single-writer lock expires mid-run; a concurrent tick reaps it and resets the tree | V3-E02 *(routine)* | — *(no story: the fix is in the routine's own files, outside this repository)* | `open` | n/a — measured from `routine-lock.sh`'s own run log | **Observed live on this run, and it nearly cost the slice.** The lock was acquired at 20:03 with `GATE=OK`; V3's Step 4 asks for a heartbeat around the *build* and nothing asks for one during **Step 3**, which has run over an hour in every run since run 9. At **21:09** a second V3 tick found the heartbeat older than `STALE_MIN` (60 min), **reaped the lock**, took it, and released it at 21:11 — so from 21:09 this run was still writing to the checkout while holding nothing, which is the pile-up the lock exists to prevent. `heartbeat` printing `no lock held` is the only signal, and by then the guarantee has already lapsed. Recovering by re-running `gate` made it worse: the salvage path `git stash`ed every tracked modification and reset the branch to `origin/main`. The work survived **only** because that salvage stash exists (`git stash pop` restored all 9 tracked files; the new files survived by being untracked). Recorded as **R-27** | G-DNC |
| PF-80 sprint workflow reports `landed: true` on a change that breaks a spec outside the directory it verified | V3-E02 *(routine)* | — *(story to be written)* | `open` | n/a — measured from run 17's Step 4 | Discovered by `S-E02-15`, and it is the reason that slice's ledger is trustworthy rather than a defect in it. The general shape: **a shared-contract edit has a blast radius the editing agent cannot see from the directory it is working in.** Two consequences, both already acted on this run — the routine must run the **whole** gate before merging and report its verdict line (**R-23**), never a stage selection; and the workflow's readiness step should run the ratchet, which is a change to `bmad/workflows/sprint.workflow.js` and belongs in its own slice | G-DNC |
| PF-01 `demo` tenant fallback | V3-E01 | **`S-E01-1a`** *(half (a) — the `ensureUser` half)* — `docs/spec/features/v3-e01/stories/S-E01-1a.md` ; half (b) forks out as **`PF-185`** | `in-progress` — **split 2026-08-14 (run 53), measured not assumed** | `apps/api/src/shared/auth/user-sync.service.spec.ts` *(new with `S-E01-1a`; before it, the tenant origin of the whole API had **no test at all** — `apps/api/src/shared/auth/` held six `*.spec.ts` and none of them this one)* | **The finding is one sentence with two addresses, and only one of them is closable today.** `apps/api/src/shared/auth/user-sync.service.ts:9` `const DEMO_TENANT_SLUG = 'demo'`, used at `:38-56`: when an authenticated Keycloak subject matches no `UserProfile` by `authProviderId` **and** none by `email`, `ensureUser` **upserts a `demo` Tenant into existence** and creates the profile inside it with `status: active`. Since ~50 files × 242 lines do `const me = await this.users.ensureUser(jwt)` and then scope on `me.tenantId`, **whatever this function returns IS the tenancy of the request** — so tenancy was assigned by a module constant, not resolved from anything trusted. Live and reachable: `ADR-004` puts every tenant in **one** realm, and `infra/keycloak/realm-export.json:106-135` ships **three enabled, unseeded identities** (`admin@`, `teacher@`, `parent@pilotage.local`), each of which silently became an `active` member of a tenant its own login created, carrying its realm-role permissions. `invite.controller.ts:130-136` already documented the behaviour as deliberate. **Half (a) closes in `S-E01-1a`:** the creation branch is deleted, an unprovisioned subject is **refused fail-closed** (`UnprovisionedUserError`, `403` — the token is valid, so a second `401` would be false and would send every portal into a refresh loop), the refusal is proven to have written **nothing** by asserting on the fake client's call record rather than only on the thrown error, and a ratchet pins `DEMO_TENANT_SLUG` out of `apps/api/src/shared/auth/**`. The blast radius is handled rather than discovered: the three realm identities are seeded into the **real** `voltaire-demo` tenant (`seed-demo.ts:260-263`, `T` at `:289`) **without `authProviderId`** — measured correction, the realm export declares **no user `id` field**, Keycloak mints it per cluster, and `seed-keycloak-users.ts:108-113` discovers it by email lookup, so the email-linking branch is the shipped path and not a guess. `DNC-10` holds: **no** bypass flag, no `NODE_ENV` leg — if local dev needs a profile, the seed provides it. **Half (b) stays open as `PF-185`** — `register.controller.ts:60,366-369` upserts the same `demo` tenant, and it cannot be closed by symmetry because a public registrant has **no tenant discriminant** | G-TENANT |
| PF-02 RLS claimed, not implemented | V3-E01 | **`S-E01-2`** *(moitié (b) close)* → **`S-E01-2b`** *(moitié (a) close PARTIELLEMENT, 2026-08-13)* → **`S-E01-1`** *(bascule de connexion)* | `in-progress` | `apps/api/src/shared/prisma/prisma.service.spec.ts` — faux client sans base ni client généré · **`scripts/rls-isolation-check.js` — preuve EXÉCUTÉE contre un vrai PostgreSQL, `RLS ISOLATION: PROVEN for the non-owner role`, exit 0** | **La moitié (b) — le tenant interpolé dans le texte SQL — est supprimée ; la moitié (a) — « RLS annoncée, pas implémentée » — reste VRAIE et c'est pourquoi la ligne n'est pas fermée.** `prisma.service.ts:29` lisait `$executeRawUnsafe("SET LOCAL app.current_tenant_id = '" + tenantId + "'")` ; le contexte part désormais en **paramètre lié** via `SELECT set_config('app.current_tenant_id', $1, true) AS applied` (template balisé `$queryRaw`), un id non-UUID canonique est **refusé avant `$transaction`** (`TenantContextError` exportée ; non-string refusé avant la regex, aucun `trim()`/casse/retrait — refus, pas assainissement), et la valeur renvoyée par `set_config` est **relue** puis comparée en `===` à l'id demandé, sinon la transaction est abandonnée et `fn` n'est jamais appelé. **Ce qui reste faux après ce diff, mesuré et non déduit :** zéro `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, zéro `CREATE POLICY` dans tout le dépôt (`0_baseline` compris) et **zéro appelant** de `withTenant` — un grep ne rend que sa propre définition. Le helper est donc la **couture d'application future**, pas un mécanisme d'isolation actif, et son docblock le dit maintenant explicitement (les deux propositions de l'ancien commentaire étaient mesurablement fausses). `ADR-032` est écrit en `accepted (partial)` : D1 paramètre lié, D2 refus fail-closed avant transaction, D3 relecture, D4 aucun contournement (DNC-10) sont **décidés et exécutés** ; la moitié policy RLS + prédicat applicatif reste **proposée**. `ADR-002` est annoté en tête et au-dessus de sa §Decision plutôt que réécrit. **Deux pièges sont consignés dans `ADR-032` comme prérequis DURS de la story RLS**, parce qu'ils sont les deux façons dont cette story livre du vert en ne protégeant rien : (1) le **propriétaire des tables contourne RLS** sans `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` — avec le montage Prisma courant le rôle applicatif possède les tables, donc policies + tests verts + isolation **nulle** ; (2) `current_setting('app.current_tenant_id')::uuid` **sans son second argument `missing_ok`** lève `42704` sur toute connexion sans contexte (et `22P02` sur chaîne vide), ce qui casse migrations, seeds, health checks et tous les jobs dès le premier jour. Fermeture de la ligne = quand une policy existe, s'applique au rôle que l'API utilise réellement, et qu'un test cross-tenant le prouve. **⚠️ Mis à jour 2026-08-13 (`S-E01-2b`) — la phrase « zéro `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, zéro `CREATE POLICY` dans tout le dépôt » ci-dessus était vraie le 2026-08-11 et NE L'EST PLUS** : elle est conservée telle quelle comme état daté, pas supprimée, mais elle ne doit plus être lue comme une mesure courante. Ce qui est vrai maintenant, mesuré et EXÉCUTÉ : la migration `20260813120000_tenant_rls_policies` pose `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` + une policy `tenant_isolation` (`FOR ALL TO PUBLIC`, `USING` ≡ `WITH CHECK`) sur les **44** tables portant `tenant_id`, avec le prédicat `nullif(current_setting('app.current_tenant_id', true), '')::uuid = tenant_id` — le `nullif` n'est pas décoratif : après un `COMMIT`, `set_config(…, true)` laisse le GUC à `''` et NON à NULL, donc un cast nu lèverait `22P02` à la **deuxième** requête de chaque connexion du pool (mesuré), et `nullif` n'est PAS un `IS NULL OR` déguisé (`''` → NULL, et NULL ne laisse passer aucune ligne). Le GRANT DML aux 44 tables n'est **pas uniforme** : `audit_log` et `conversation_message` ne reçoivent que `SELECT, INSERT`, sinon la chaîne de hachage de l'audit deviendrait réinscriptible par le rôle applicatif au moment de la bascule (GUARDRAILS §1, ADR-037 §D4) ; `conversation_report` est exclu de cette exemption **nommément et avec sa raison** (la modération mute `status`/`reviewed_by`). `scripts/rls-isolation-check.js` (stage 600 de `ci-gate.sh`, étape de `ci.yml`, aucun skip — DNC-08) le PROUVE par exécution : base scratch, ledger complet appliqué, connecté **comme `app_user`** (0 table possédée, pas de `BYPASSRLS`), **contrôle positif d'abord** (les lignes APPARAISSENT sous GUC=A avant de disparaître sous GUC=B — `app_user` n'avait aucun privilège avant, donc une preuve montrant seulement l'absence aurait été verte pour la mauvaise raison), `INSERT` étranger refusé par `WITH CHECK`, `UPDATE` cross-tenant refusé, cas `22P02` du pool, recensement des privilèges append-only, rollback EXÉCUTÉ. Le panel a injecté **5 mutants dans la migration : 5 tués**. **La ligne reste `in-progress`, et la raison est précise : l'application qui tourne n'est TOUJOURS PAS isolée par RLS.** Elle se connecte comme `pilotage`, propriétaire des 55 tables ; `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` est **délibérément absent** (le poser aujourd'hui rendrait zéro ligne à toutes les requêtes de tous les portails) ; `withTenant` a toujours **zéro appelant** ; et six tables dérivées par clé étrangère n'ont **aucune policy** (`grade_revision`, `announcement_receipt`, `branding`, `import_row`, `user_role`, `outbox_event`). **Condition de fermeture RESTANTE, restreinte à une seule chose : la BASCULE DE CONNEXION** `DATABASE_URL` → `app_user` (avec le complément de GRANT sur les tables rôle/permission/tenant, délibérément omises ici), plus la décision sur les six tables dérivées. Elle appartient à `S-E01-1`. Décisions consignées dans **`ADR-032` §D5–§D8**, avec §Deferred item 1/2 annotés sur place — pas dans un commentaire de migration. **⚠️ Mis à jour 2026-08-15 (run 61, `S-E01-1e`) — la moitié (a) avance d'un DEUXIÈME module, et le compteur qui la mesure a été RÉPARÉ avant de bouger.** `lessons` est converti (5 handlers, `this.scope.run(...)` lexicalement dans chacun, `tx` à l'intérieur), et l'attribution est **re-dérivée, jamais éditée en littéral** : `13 scoped + 111 enumerated / 800` → **`24 scoped + 120 enumerated / 803`**, donc **659** sites d'appel rendraient ZÉRO LIGNE après la bascule. **L'application n'est toujours PAS prête à basculer, et la suite le dit comme une LIMITE nommée.** La réparation compte plus que le mouvement : jusqu'à cette tranche, `PRISMA_CALL_SITE_RE` traitait `prisma.`, `this.prisma.` et `tx.` à l'identique et `covers()` était purement POSITIONNEL, donc un `this.prisma.` **à l'intérieur** d'un callback de portée comptait comme *scoped* — l'inverse dangereux de `PF-200`, où l'instruction tourne sur la connexion PROPRIÉTAIRE pendant que le compteur la crédite à la portée. **Un handler à moitié converti produisait donc un compte SUPÉRIEUR à un handler correct.** `SCOPE_SAFE_RECEIVERS = ['tx']` et `classifyCallSite` (fonction PURE, quatre issues dont `owner-inside-scope`) ferment cela, et l'ordre porte la propriété : le test du receveur passe AVANT l'énumération, donc un site couvert ne peut pas être blanchi par un fichier allow-listé (`ADR-051 §D1`)  **⚠️ Mis à jour 2026-08-22 (run 66, `S-E01-1h`) — la moitié (a) avance SANS que le compteur bouge, et c'est la première fois que cette ligne peut le dire.** Le compteur est re-dérivé, identique à `main` : `36 scoped + 120 enumerated / 816`. Ce qui a changé n'est pas l'arbre source mais la CIBLE : `DATABASE_URL_APP` n'était déclarée dans AUCUN service de `infra/docker-compose.yml`, donc `AppRolePrismaService` n'ouvrait jamais sa seconde connexion et **les 36 sites déjà convertis s'exécutaient sur la connexion du PROPRIÉTAIRE** (`pilotage`, `rolbypassrls = true` mesuré), dans l'état `degraded_no_app_url`. La base de la pile était en outre à **2 migrations sur 7**, avec **0** policy et **0** privilège `app_user`. **Les preuves de `S-E01-1d`/`1e`/`1g` étaient VRAIES et prouvaient le MÉCANISME sur une base scratch ; aucune ne prouvait le DÉPLOIEMENT** — or la condition de fermeture restante de cette ligne (`ADR-032` §D5–§D8) porte précisément sur la connexion. Après `S-E01-1h`, EXÉCUTÉ sur la pile locale : 7/7 migrations, 199 grants, 53 tables RLS, 59 policies, clôture `25/25` DÉTENUE (comparaison d'ensembles, les deux côtés dérivés — jamais un littéral, `project_paired_lists_drift`), état **`enforced`**, jauge `pilotage_tenant_scope_enforced{app="api"} 1`, 229 routes montées, 0 erreur. Refus prouvé sur la base de la pile, CONTRÔLE POSITIF D'ABORD — obligatoire ici, parce qu'`app_user` ne détenait AUCUN privilège avant, donc une preuve ne montrant qu'une absence de lignes aurait été verte pour la mauvaise raison (elle aurait mesuré un GRANT manquant, pas une policy qui fonctionne) : propriétaire + GUC étranger → **17 lignes**, `app_user` + GUC propre → **17**, `app_user` + GUC étranger → **0**, `app_user` sans GUC → **0**. La première ligne EST le défaut énoncé en nombre : l'API était cette connexion. **La ligne reste `in-progress` :** 660 sites rendraient toujours ZÉRO LIGNE après la bascule `DATABASE_URL`, l'application n'est toujours PAS prête à basculer, et rien de tout cela n'est revendiqué pour l'hôte Hostinger, qui n'a pas été contacté (Step −1). `ADR-056`. **⚠️ Mis à jour 2026-08-22 (run 67, `S-E01-1i`) — la moitié (a) avance d’un QUATRIÈME module, et pour la première fois le numérateur monte sans que le dénominateur bouge.** `student-portal` est converti sur la TOTALITÉ de ses onze sites d’appel : attribution re-dérivée par `scripts/tenant-adversarial-check.js`, `36 scoped + 120 enumerated / 818` → **`47 scoped + 120 enumerated / 818`**, donc **651** sites rendraient encore ZÉRO LIGNE après la bascule (662 avant). Le dénominateur INCHANGÉ est le point : `resolveSelf` est partagé par sept handlers, et l’inliner aurait donné `51 / 824` — une couverture qui monte parce que le corpus grossit. La portée est donc ouverte DANS le helper (un seul site, appelé sept fois), décidé en `ADR-057 §D2` avec le tableau des trois formes. **Premier module converti qui ne DÉTIENT plus de client propriétaire :** `PrismaService` n’est plus injecté dans `StudentPortalService`, ce qui n’est pas une convention de revue mais une propriété du constructeur (`ADR-057 §D4`) — les trois modules précédents en gardent tous un. **La clôture de privilèges a été SOUS-COMPTÉE par le brief, et l’écart est le second résultat du run :** `NEXT.md` (run 66) dimensionnait la tranche à trois nouveaux droits (les tables RACINES des `findMany`) ; il en faut **cinq**, parce que sous RLS une relation TRAVERSÉE par un `select` imbriqué est une table LUE — `/student/grades` descend `grade -> assessment -> term`, donc `assessment` et `term` sont dues et manquaient. Les cinq ont été vérifiées DÉTENUES sur la base de la pile AVANT d’être déclarées, jamais après. `APP_ROLE_REQUIRED_PRIVILEGES` : **25 → 30**, et `scripts/tenant-scope-check.js` re-lance la sonde contre le **dist recompilé** (le script charge le seam COMPILÉ, jamais la source — la première exécution a rendu `25`, ce qui est la preuve que l’artefact et non le fichier est mesuré) : *« AC-2.1 the 30 privileges the converted module needs are HELD — all held »*, verdict `enforcing`, `TENANT SCOPE: PROVEN`. **Refus EXÉCUTÉ sur la base de la PILE (la cible du Step −1), contrôle positif d’abord**, comme `app_user` (`rolbypassrls = f`, mesuré et non déduit) : GUC = `voltaire-demo` → `student 2463`, `enrollment 2463`, `grade 420`, `student_subject_snapshot 840`, `assessment 16`, `term 3` ; GUC = `demo` (étranger) → **0** partout **sauf `term`, qui rend 3** ; sans GUC → **0**. Le `3` de `term` a d’abord été instruit comme une fuite puis **mesuré comme le contraire** : `demo` possède ses PROPRES 3 trimestres, et sous chaque GUC `string_agg(distinct tenant_id)` ne rend que le tenant demandé. `term` est donc le contrôle le PLUS FORT du lot — la seule table où les deux tenants portent des lignes, donc la seule qui prouve que la policy **sélectionne** au lieu de rendre vide. Côté propriétaire, le trou est réaffirmé en nombres : `pilotage` (`rolbypassrls = true`) sous GUC `demo` voit **420 notes et 2463 élèves** étrangers. **Limite nommée plutôt que tue :** `attendance_record` et `announcement_receipt` portent **0 ligne** sur cette pile, donc leur refus n’est prouvé que par la suite adversariale (qui sème ses propres fixtures), pas par cette sonde. **La ligne reste `in-progress` :** 651 sites d’appel rendraient toujours zéro ligne après la bascule, `AnalyticsService`, `RemediationService` et `StudentAccessService` restent sur la connexion du propriétaire (et ne sont délibérément PAS ajoutés à `ENUMERATED_OUTSIDE_SCOPE`, `ADR-048 §D6`), et rien n’est revendiqué pour Hostinger, non contacté. `ADR-057`. | G-TENANT, G-DNC |
| PF-247 the ADR-058 §D1 abort-recovery rule is provable only LEXICALLY — no fake-client test can fail on it | V3-E01 | — *(recorded by `S-E01-1j`, which both introduced the rule and ratcheted the half that can be ratcheted)* | `open` | `apps/api/src/modules/remediation/remediation-scope-ownership.spec.ts` — the three source-text assertions + the "no `catch` inside a scope callback" ratchet | **P2, and the reason it is a finding rather than a solved problem is the shape of the proof, not the state of the code.** `TenantScopeService.run` opens an **interactive** transaction: any error aborts it and every later statement on that `tx` raises `25P02`. So a `catch` may not issue a Prisma statement inside the scope whose statement threw — `ADR-058 §D1`. `remediation.service.ts` has **three** such recoveries (`promotePlan`'s P2002 → winner re-read, `reopenPlan`'s P2002 → `'conflict_open_exists'`, `readSubjectAverage`'s snapshot failure → live fall-through), and all three are written correctly. **What cannot be mechanised:** a unit test's fake `run` calls `fn(client)` and opens no transaction, so nothing can be aborted and the wrong shape passes every fake-client test that exists. The spec therefore asserts the property on the **source text** (the `try {` byte index precedes the scope-opening index; the `catch` follows; no scope callback contains the token `catch`). That ratchet fires on the *first* attempt at the dangerous form, which is worth having — but it is lexical, and the hazard is transactional: a recovery written in a helper the scope calls, or through an intermediate function, is invisible to it. **Fix direction (not this slice):** an integration test against a real PostgreSQL that forces a P2002 inside a scope and asserts the recovery returns the row rather than `25P02` — i.e. the only place the property is genuinely observable. Until then it is a **review obligation**, and this row is where it is written down. `ADR-058 §D1` | G-TENANT |
| PF-248 three fail-soft `catch` blocks now swallow scope refusals and permission errors, and `promotePlan` PERSISTS the damage | V3-E01 | — *(story to be written; found by `S-E01-1j` while converting the module, recorded rather than fixed because the slice's own non-regression AC required byte-identical behaviour)* | `open` | n/a — read from `remediation.service.ts` (`readSubjectAverage`, `computeLiveSubjectBaseline`, `isTeacherOfStudent`) and from both consumers | **P1, and the severity is in the asymmetry between the three catches, not in any one of them.** All three catch **everything** and degrade quietly: the two baseline readers return `{avg:null,trendDelta:null}` at `logger.debug`, and the E2 teaching wall returns `false` at `logger.error`. That was harmless while every statement ran on the owner connection. After `S-E01-1j` the likeliest new error inside them is a `ServiceUnavailableException` from `AppRolePrismaService.transactionRunnerOrNull()` in state `refused_unusable`, or a `42501` from a missing grant — i.e. an **infrastructure** fault. Consequences, in order of how hard they are to undo: (1) `captureSubjectBaseline` runs at promote time and its result is **written into `remediation_plan.baseline_avg` once, forever** — during a misconfiguration window every plan promoted is created with a permanently null baseline, the S3 strip shows "en attente" for the life of the plan, and no later run repairs it; (2) `isTeacherOfStudent` is a **WALL** — an infrastructure fault renders as "this teacher does not teach this student", a 403 indistinguishable from a real ABAC denial (fail-closed, so not a leak, but undiagnosable); (3) both dashboard consumers (`analytics.service.ts:1207`, `student-portal.service.ts:603`) wrap `remediationProgress` in `try/catch → []` at `logger.debug`, so the same fault renders as **"this child has no remediation plan"** on the parent strip and student block C — a wrong statement, invisible at the default log level. This is the `attendance_record` "reads as data, not as a panne" hazard of `S-E01-1i`, on the module's highest-traffic surface. **Fix direction:** narrow all three catches to rethrow `TenantScopeError`, `ServiceUnavailableException` and Prisma permission/connection errors (`42501`, `P2010`, initialization), letting only a genuine data miss degrade; and raise the two consumers' degrade log to error/warn when the cause is a scope failure. Two targeted tests: a fake scope whose `run()` throws 503 must make `promotePlan` **propagate** rather than create a null-baseline plan, and must make `isTeacherOfStudent` throw rather than answer `false`. `ADR-058` "Conséquences" | G-TENANT |
| TOOL-38 the host's `localhost:5432` is a DECOY database that reads exactly like "RLS is unverifiable here" | V3-E01 *(routine)* | — *(recorded by `S-E01-1j`; it is an environment fact, not a code defect)* | `open` | n/a — measured this run: `docker port pilotage_postgres` returns **nothing** | **P3 in severity, P1 in wasted time, which is why it is written down.** The stack database lives **inside** `pilotage_postgres`, which publishes **no** host port — it is reachable only through `docker exec pilotage_postgres psql -U pilotage -d pilotage …`. The host's `localhost:5432` is the **native Windows service** `postgresql-x64-15`: a different database with 55 tables but only two migrations applied (`0_baseline`, `20260809120000_tenant_timezone`), so the RLS migration is **absent** there — it shows **0 tables with RLS, 0 grants to `app_user`, and 0 rows in every table including `tenant`**. Two wrong conclusions are one command away: measuring the closure there and reporting "unverifiable / no evidence available", or running `migrate deploy` / `db push` there to "fix" it (forbidden — it is not the stack's database and never was). **Mitigation until someone publishes the port:** every tenant-isolation probe in this programme must go through `docker exec`, and any probe that reports zero rows in `tenant` is measuring the decoy, not the stack | — |
| TOOL-39 an unclosed scope-opening token inside a DOCBLOCK silently zeroes a whole file's tenant-scope coverage | V3-E02 *(routine)* | — *(recorded by `S-E01-1j`; the checker behaved as designed — the cost is that its symptom is indistinguishable from "the conversion did not land")* | `open` | n/a — observed live this run, then removed and re-measured | **P3, and it is a fail-closed design whose *symptom* is the problem.** `scopeCallbackRanges` in `scripts/tenant-adversarial-check.js` matches `<receiver>.run(` over the **whole file text**, comments included, and brace-matches forward. A docblock line reading ``…AVANT `await this.scope.run(` …`` — prose, naming the call, with an open parenthesis and no closing one — therefore starts a range that never closes; the file is counted **`unbalanced`**, and by the fail-closed rule **every** call site in it contributes **zero** coverage. Measured live: the first attribution run after converting all 23 sites printed an unchanged **`47 scoped / 818`**, i.e. exactly the number that means "nothing landed". The checker **does** name the file in a `[FAIL]` line, so it is diagnostic rather than mysterious — but the headline counter it also prints says the opposite, and the counter is what gets copied into a ledger. Rewriting the prose as `this.scope.run(…)` restored the true **70**. **Fix direction:** strip comments before scanning (the scanner already carries a regex-literal heuristic for the same class of reason), or report `unbalanced` files *in* the readiness line rather than only in a separate stage | — |

Deleting a grant on the finding alone is a silent, boot-green, runtime-`42501` outage on all four portals — hence `dead-entry-advisory`, which **fails** the check but states a removal bar of a **measured** negative. **What stays open:** the trigger and the derivation's corpus are still **two hand-maintained lists** — `cutoverReadiness` walks `apps/api/src` **and `apps/worker/src`** (125 Prisma call sites today), while the trigger names neither `apps/worker/src` nor `apps/api/src/**` outside `modules/` and `shared/prisma/`. A worker conversion still lands green. Derive the trigger from the derivation's roots, or add the missing roots. | G-TENANT |

A derivation that cannot follow a hoisted `const NAME = { … }` derives nothing for those sites, and because the comparison runs in both directions the blind spot presents as a `dead-entry-advisory` — *an invitation to delete a grant the runtime needs*, which is the worst-shaped finding this gate can emit. The resolver ships and is tested. **What stays open, and it is the one fail-OPEN branch in a loudly fail-closed walker:** `resolveValues` (`scripts/tenant-adversarial-check.js:3113`) returns `[]` with **no `note()`** when a constant has already been visited at the same call site. `seenConsts` is keyed on the constant NAME only, so one hoisted constant referenced twice at a call site against **different** models loses the second traversal silently. Reproduced on a fixture: `select: SEL` + `include: { beta: { select: SEL } }` derives `alpha`/`beta`/`gamma` but **not** `delta`, with zero problems reported — under-derivation that produces no finding at all, i.e. a missing pair reaching boot unnamed. | PF-258 a scope refusal swallowed by `resolveMeetingAssignee` would PERSIST as `assignedToId: null` — a config fault written into the data | V3-E01 | **`S-E01-1l`** — recorded and **FIXED in the same diff**; `ADR-060 §D7` | ~~`open`~~ **`closed`** | `apps/api/src/modules/alerts/alerts.service.ts` — the method-wide `catch` now RETHROWS `TenantScopeError` and `ServiceUnavailableException` and degrades only on what could already fail; targeted test in `alerts.service.spec.ts` (« un refus de portée (503) REMONTE au lieu de créer une demande non assignée ») | **P2, and it is a hazard THIS conversion introduced rather than inherited.** `resolveMeetingAssignee` degrades to `null` on any error, and that degradation is PERSISTED: `assignedToId: null` is written once, forever — the teacher never sees the request, it lands in the admin unassigned bucket, and nothing records why. | PF-259 `AlertsService.tenantsWithEnabledRules()` is a cross-tenant read with NO caller in `apps/api` or `apps/worker` | V3-E01 | — *(recorded by `S-E01-1l`; enumerated honestly rather than excused or deleted)* | `open` | `grep -rn tenantsWithEnabledRules apps/` → one hit, the definition itself (measured 2026-08-23); the worker cron carries its own mirror at `apps/worker/src/modules/alerts-cron/alerts-evaluator.service.ts` | **P3.** `alerts.service.ts` issues `alertRule.findMany({ select:{tenantId:true}, distinct:['tenantId'] })` with **no tenant predicate**. It cannot enter a scope by construction — it PRODUCES the tenant set a scope would key on — so it is enumerated in `ENUMERATED_OUTSIDE_SCOPE` with exactly that reason, plus the measured fact that nothing calls it. **Why enumerated and not deleted:** removing a public method of an exported service is not a scope conversion, and the enumeration reason field is the only thing keeping that list honest — writing « bootstrap/scheduler » would have been a lie. **Fix direction:** decide whether the worker mirror should call this instead of duplicating it, or delete the method in a slice that owns the worker seam. | G-TENANT |
| PF-260 `PRISMA_CALL_SITE_RE`'s negative lookbehind makes property-chain receivers such as `ctx.prisma` INVISIBLE to the AC-9 census and to the verb-aware grant classifier | V3-E01 *(gate integrity)* | — *(recorded by `S-E01-1l`, verified by measurement before filing)* | `open` | none — the measurement IS the evidence: `grep -roh "ctx\.prisma\.[A-Za-z]*\.[A-Za-z]*" apps/api/src` returns **8** production sites (6 × `grade.findMany`, 1 × `attendanceRecord.groupBy`, 1 × `student.findMany`), none of which appears in the 818-site corpus | **P2.** `PRISMA_CALL_SITE_RE` anchors on `(?<![.\w])(?:prisma\|this\.prisma\|tx)`, so a receiver reached through a property chain — `ctx.prisma` in `apps/api/src/modules/alerts/rules/*.rule.ts` — is skipped entirely. Two consequences, both understated by the current green lines: the **denominator under-counts** (818 is not the corpus, it is the corpus the matcher can see), and « no production Prisma call site reaches a table ungranted to app_user » is **narrower than it reads** — those 8 sites hit `grade`, `attendance_record` and `student` and are never checked against `role_table_grants`. It also means a future conversion of `evaluateAll` could leave 8 owner-connection statements inside an open scope with **no** `owner-inside-scope` report. **Fix direction:** widen `PRISMA_RECEIVERS` to allow a bounded property chain and REPORT every new receiver it discovers rather than absorbing it — the discipline `foreignReceivers` already applies to transaction aliases. | G-TENANT |
| PF-261 `ensureRules` can create duplicate tenant-level rules, and a plain `GET /alerts/rules` performs an INSERT | V3-E01 | — *(recorded by `S-E01-1l`; PRE-EXISTING, not caused by the conversion)* | `open` | none — read from `apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma` (`@@unique([tenantId, schoolId, code])` with `schoolId String?`) and `apps/api/src/modules/alerts/alerts.service.ts` (`createMany` without `skipDuplicates`) | **P2 for the duplicate, P3 for the write-on-read.** PostgreSQL treats NULLs as DISTINCT in a unique index and Prisma emits no `NULLS NOT DISTINCT`, so for **tenant-level** rules (`schoolId = null`) the compound unique **does not fire**. `ensureRules` is invoked from `listRules` AND `updateRule`, so two concurrent first-loads on a fresh tenant can yield 2×8 duplicate rows; `updateRule` then `findFirst`s an arbitrary one and `listRules` keeps whichever wins its `Map`. The second half is a shape problem: a **read** endpoint opens a write transaction on `app_user` and derives `alert_rule.INSERT` from a read path — correct for the closure, wrong as a product shape, and it is what makes the duplicate reachable from an ordinary page load. **Fix direction:** `skipDuplicates: true` plus a partial unique index on `(tenantId, code) WHERE school_id IS NULL` — a migration, therefore its own slice AND a `scripts/restore-drill-baseline.json` entry (PF-80) — and move materialisation off the GET. | G-TENANT |
| TOOL-41 the ADR-058 §D1 abort-recovery ratchet is PER-MODULE and copied by hand, so the module that forgets it is the one that needs it | V3-E01 *(routine)* | — *(recorded by `S-E01-1l`, which added the second copy rather than the shared check)* | `open` | `apps/api/src/modules/alerts/alerts-scope-ownership.spec.ts` (this slice) and `apps/api/src/modules/remediation/remediation-scope-ownership.spec.ts` — and NOTHING for `student-portal`, the fifth converted module, which has no `*-scope-ownership.spec.ts` at all | **P2, and the multiplicity is what changes the priority.** The « no scope callback contains the token `catch` » ratchet lives in a per-module spec that reads a single hard-coded SOURCE. `remediation` has three swallow-and-continue sites; `alerts` adds **six**, so from this slice on the MAJORITY of the rule's instances sit in a module whose ratchet exists only because its author remembered to write one — the exact reproach ADR-035 makes to interceptors (« un endroit qu'un auteur futur oublie d'appliquer »). `student-portal` is the proof it is already happening. **Fix direction:** hoist the lexical assertions into a corpus-wide check under `apps/api/src/shared/quality/` driven by every file containing `scope.run(`, so adding a module to the converted set IS adding it to the ratchet — rather than copying a per-module spec a third time. | G-TENANT |
| TOOL-40 the closure comparison is pure source-to-source yet cannot be driven without a database, and a green boot probe is NOT a backstop for a wrong derivation | V3-E01 *(routine)* | — *(recorded by `S-E01-1k`; the new pure functions ARE exported, the pre-existing three are not)* | `open` | partial — `derivePrivilegeClosure`, `privilegeClosureDrift`, `parseAppRoleRequiredPrivileges`, `parsePrismaSchema`, `parseDerivedChildParents` are exported and driven on synthetic input with no repository and no database | **P2, two faces, both cited from checked-in code (`tenant-adversarial-check.js:2735`, `:3767`, `:4652`).** *(a) The held-probe is not a backstop.* A derivation without positional attribution emits `booking.INSERT`, `booking.UPDATE` and `audit_log.INSERT` as phantoms; `audit_log` **holds** `INSERT` (measured), so that phantom would boot **GREEN** and be dead forever. Correctness of the derivation cannot be recovered from the grant probe — which is why the attribution refusal is asserted rather than narrated. *(b) A check coupled to a running database cannot run where the drift is introduced.* The whole closure comparison — `parsePrismaSchema` → `derivePrivilegeClosure` → `privilegeClosureDrift` — reads **only source bytes**, yet it is computed inside `cutoverReadiness()` and called from `main()` **after** the database census, in a stage that throws `ToolingUnavailable` → **exit 1** for a missing `psql`, a missing `DATABASE_URL_APP` or a closed TCP port (`DNC-08`: no skip, by design). `ADR-059 §D3` just made that DB-bearing path the **common** path on backend diffs, and this stage is the known-intermittent one on this host (`AC-P16`, `TOOL-36`) — so *"Postgres is down"* and *"tenancy is broken"* are now the same gate colour. **Fix, ~0 s and already pure:** split the source-to-source comparison into its own `run_stage` carrying the wide `apps/api/src/modules/` trigger, and leave the narrow prisma/identity-seam trigger on the 300 s adversarial DB suite; only the call site in `main()` moves. *(c) Cheap follow-up, unchanged from the story's §10:* `cutoverReadiness`, `sourceFiles` and `PRISMA_CALL_SITE_RE` are still absent from `module.exports`, so a reviewer wanting to drive the derivation over the real corpus must re-walk it by hand — which is how a second, drifting copy of the matcher gets born (it happened during this slice's own review). | G-TENANT |
| PF-262 the audit-vocabulary extractor resolves a forwarded parameter only in the INNERMOST function, so wrapping a write in a tenant-scope callback SILENTLY drops its codes | V3-E01 *(gate integrity)* | **`S-E01-1l`** — recorded and **FIXED at land by the routine**, on the regression it had itself caused | ~~`open`~~ **`closed`** | `apps/api/src/shared/quality/audit-vocabulary-gate.spec.ts` — `resolveExpression` now walks OUTWARD through enclosing functions until one DECLARES the root name; **93/93 pass** across that file and `remediation-scope-ownership.spec.ts` (EXECUTED `npx jest`, 2026-08-23), where 3 were RED before | **P1, and it is the first defect this epic has produced whose symptom is an instruction to DELETE working vocabulary.** `resolveExpression` called `enclosingFunction(expr)` **once** and gave up when that function declared no parameter named `args`. The assumption held only while every `auditLog.create` sat directly in the body of the helper whose parameter it forwards. `S-E01-1l` wraps the write in `this.scope.run(tenantId, async (tx) => …)`, so the innermost function became the **arrow** — sole parameter `tx` — and `args.action` stopped resolving. It did not fail loudly: `parameterInfo` returned `undefined`, the visitor returned early, and `alert.acknowledge` / `alert.resolve` / `alert.dismiss` left `WRITTEN_ACTIONS` **with no diagnostic at all**. The REVERSE completeness direction then reported all three as **dead action labels** — a green-looking instruction to delete vocabulary the runtime still writes, the same worst-shaped-finding class as `PF-250`. Caught ONLY because `ci-gate.sh` names its failing tests. **The fix is lexical and belongs in the extractor, not in the shape of the converted service:** a closure referencing `args` resolves it in an enclosing scope, which is what TypeScript itself does; the walk stops at the first declaring function, so shadowing is preserved. **Every remaining module conversion crosses this same boundary** — the extractor would have mis-read each one. | G-AUDIT |
| PF-263 a MODULE-LOCAL ownership guard asserted on the GLOBAL declared closure, so another module's LEGITIMATE declaration turns it red | V3-E01 *(gate integrity)* | **`S-E01-1l`** — recorded and **FIXED at land by the routine** | ~~`open`~~ **`closed`** | `apps/api/src/modules/remediation/remediation-scope-ownership.spec.ts` — the inverse guard now derives from `sites` (this file's OWN call sites) instead of `declared`; EXECUTED 2026-08-23 | **P2, and it is a FALSE RED — the direction that teaches a routine to distrust its own gates.** `AC-5`'s inverse guard is named « aucune mutation d'alerte », a claim about **remediation**, but asserted `declared.has('alert_instance.UPDATE') === false`, where `declared` is `APP_ROLE_REQUIRED_PRIVILEGES` — the closure shared by **every** converted module. The two coincided only while remediation was the sole module touching those tables. Converting `alerts` declared `alert_instance.UPDATE` for acknowledge / resolve / dismiss — correctly, derived, and required — and turned this assertion RED **without one line of remediation changing**. The adjacent `derivedDeletes` half of the very same test already measured the right thing (`sites`, not `declared`); the two halves disagreed about what they were asserting. **Why this earns an id rather than a one-line edit:** four sibling `*-scope-ownership.spec.ts` files exist (`announcements`, `calendar`, `lessons`, `remediation`) and `TOOL-41` already records that the family is copied by hand. Any of them reading `declared` for a module-local claim carries the same latent false red, and the NEXT conversion is what fires it. | G-TENANT |

| PF-225 the OIDC ratchet has **no executable negative control of its own** — gutting its wildcard rules leaves it green | V3-E01 | **S-E01-4b** *(the slice that built the ratchet; the gap is in the ratchet's own tests)* | `open` | `apps/api/src/shared/quality/keycloak-client-identity-gate.spec.ts` — **20/20 pass, and still 20/20 with the rules disabled** | **EXECUTED at land, not argued.** With `wildcardProblems()` replaced by `return []` — disabling `W-1`, `W-2` **and** `W-3`, the primary security content of `scripts/keycloak-client-check.js` — `node scripts/keycloak-client-check.js` still exits **0** and the spec still reports **20/20**. Five of the seven new `it()` blocks are `readFileSync` + `toContain` over source text; **none** executes `auditRealm` / `auditAccessor` / `auditProvisioner` / `wildcardProblems` against a mutated input. The four `AC-5` controls exist only as one-time transcripts in `ADR-052 §Evidence`. **This is `PF-18`'s own failure mode — a rule at an address where nothing checks it — reproduced inside the checker built to prevent it**, and it matters more than usual because the stage is unconditional and blocking on every PR. Fix: append in-memory mutation controls driven through `auditRealm(rule, realm)` (positive control first, then delete `portal-student`, add `/student/*` to `portal-parent` asserting the by-name refusal, wildcard a callback segment, make `portal-teacher` public), and give `deriveRule()` an optional `{ accessorSource, portalsSource }` override so the accessor-alias control — `PF-18` itself — can be re-run instead of staying prose | G-AUTHZ |
| PF-226 the ratchet's coverage is narrower than its PASS banner claims — absent flags, origins and non-`.mjs` carriers are invisible | V3-E01 | **S-E01-4b** *(recorded at land; the gate is a net gain, this is its ceiling)* | `open` | n/a — traced and, for the flag cases, executed against in-memory clones of `infra/keycloak/realm-export.json` | Four blind spots in `scripts/keycloak-client-check.js`, none of them a regression — nothing that passed before fails now, but the banner over-claims. (a) `auditRealm` asserts the three flags a portal client MUST hold and none it must NOT: `enabled: false` passes (a byte-for-byte reproduction of the `S-E01-4a` symptom — student login dead — landing green), and so does `implicitFlowEnabled: true` on a client already carrying `/<portal>/*`, which is token-in-fragment delivery. (b) `uriPath()` keeps only `pathname`, so scheme/host/port are invisible to every closure rule and a foreign-origin redirect URI passes; an origin-**homogeneity** rule would catch it without hard-coding a port. (c) `byId` is last-wins while the closure loop walks the array, so a duplicate `portal-student` entry inserted first is masked; a `clientId: ""` entry is `continue`d rather than failed, against `DNC-08`. (d) `auditInfraCoverage()` walks `infra/` **non-recursively** and only for `.mjs`/`.js`, so `infra/docker-compose.yml` — the third hand-maintained portal→client list this very story names — can never be seen by it, and neither can `infra/keycloak/*.mjs` | G-AUTHZ |
| PF-227 `scripts/keycloak-live-probe.js` plants a callback wildcard behind a hostname check and never removes it | V3-E01 | **S-E01-4b** *(the file shipped written and NEVER EXECUTED — fix before it is ever run)* | `open` | **none — the script has never been executed** (`docker info` → exit 1 at land) | `assertLoopback()` accepts any URL whose hostname is `localhost` / `127.0.0.1` / `::1`. **Loopback is a property of the socket, not of the realm**: an operator with `ssh -L 8081:keycloak:8080` open to the VPS — the normal way to reach an admin console that is not publicly exposed, and this repo's deploy story is exactly SSH-to-Hostinger — satisfies the guard while pointing at **production**. The probe then (1) PUTs `${BASE}/api/auth/callback/*` onto `portal-parent` and `portal-student` (the `PF-209` shape, deliberately reintroduced), (2) runs the **production** provisioner with `PUBLIC_BASE_URL` defaulting to `http://localhost:3000`, overwriting `rootUrl`/`baseUrl`/`redirectUris`/`webOrigins` on all four clients — bypassing the provisioner's own empty-URL safety valve — and (3) deletes the ephemeral user in `finally` but **never restores the planted wildcard**, which the provisioner's `exit 1` on a missing client can make permanent. Fix: require an explicit `KC_PROBE_DESTRUCTIVE=1` **and** read a disposability signal back from the realm (e.g. the client secrets still equal the `change-me-portal-*` placeholders a real deployment necessarily rotates), then wrap the plant in its own `finally`. Secondary: `:326` mints the parent token with a hard-coded `parent123` that exists nowhere else in the repo and cannot satisfy the realm's own `passwordPolicy`, so the parent side of the `azp` proof can never be observed — use a second ephemeral identity | G-AUTHZ |

Net: **a `school_admin` can now assign none of the four other seeded system roles** — `super_admin` correctly (that is `PF-156`'s point) and `teacher`/`parent`/`student` as collateral, on the admin onboarding path. **The ceiling was deliberately not weakened to preserve the old behaviour**, because the old behaviour *is* the P0: the same mechanism that let an admin hand out `teacher` let them hand themselves `grades.revise`. The answer is a delegation model — an explicit assignable-roles grant, or a grantor-relative realm-role ladder permitting `school_admin → teacher\|parent\|student` while refusing `teacher → school_admin` — which also closes `PF-09`'s residual. **Needs a human ruling before implementation**; recorded in `open-decisions.md` as `D-12` | G-AUTHZ |

| PF-324 the api `test-ratchet` stage turns a GREEN test run into `GATE: FAIL` when jest cannot write its own report file | V3-E02 *(gate integrity)* | — *(story to be written; `scripts/test-ratchet.js` runJest)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-23 (run 78), by running the same thing twice** | `bash scripts/ci-gate.sh` returned `GATE: FAIL (1 stage(s))` on `test:api (ratchet)` with **`Error: ENOENT: no such file or directory, open (TEMP)/ratchet-XhoeON/jest.json`** thrown inside jest’s own `processResults` — i.e. the scratch directory `test-ratchet.js:140` created with `mkdtempSync` had **vanished before jest reached the end of the run**. The tests themselves were fine: that run printed `Tests: 11 failed, 3669 passed`, and `node scripts/test-ratchet.js api` re-run alone on the identical tree printed `3669/3680 passed · 11 failing · 11 known-failing (baseline)` and `✓ no drift`, **exit 0** — same counts, opposite verdict. **No spec explains it:** every `rmSync` under `apps/**/*.spec.ts` targets its own scratch, and nothing in either app reads or sweeps `tmpdir()` by prefix or age, so this is not the `TOOL-17` probe-file race at a new address — it is an external mutation of `TEMP` during a 380 s run. **P2, and specifically NOT cosmetic:** the failure mode is *a false RED on a correct diff at the merge gate*, the exact class `TOOL-15`/`TOOL-17`/`TOOL-18` were opened for, and the shape most likely to be “fixed” by someone weakening a real check. Candidate fixes, cheapest first: put the report file under the app’s own `node_modules/.cache` instead of `tmpdir()`; or have `runJest` re-create `scratch` and re-run once on an ENOENT that names its own scratch path, refusing on any other error (the four-step tolerance `scripts/lib/walk-read.js` already models). **Do not widen it into a bare try/catch** — that would convert an unclassifiable state into a PASS (`DNC-08`) | G-DNC |
| PF-325 `tenant-scope-deployment-check.js` prints the harness’s own verdict token, so a reader can match a SUB-STAGE and conclude the whole gate passed | V3-E02 *(gate integrity)* | — *(story to be written; a one-line prefix change plus the AC that pins it)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-23 (run 78), by being caught by it** | `scripts/tenant-scope-deployment-check.js:261` emits a bare `GATE: PASS` (and `:241`/`:256` bare `GATE: FAIL …`) — **byte-identical to the token `scripts/ci-gate.sh` uses for its FINAL verdict**. In this run’s second full gate that line landed at **line 84 of 1171**, roughly 700 s before the run ended, and a watcher grepping `GATE: (PASS|FAIL)` matched it and reported the gate green **while `test:api (ratchet)` had not yet started**. Every sibling check script prefixes its own verdict with its own name — `KEYCLOAK CLIENT CHECK: PASS`, `SCHEMA DRIFT CHECK: PASS` — so this one is the outlier, not the convention. **P2, and it attacks a rule the ledger already relies on:** `project_ci_gate_always_fails` instructs readers to trust *the printed verdict line* over `0` precisely because a piped `0` lies, and this makes the printed line ambiguous too. Fix: prefix it (`TENANT SCOPE DEPLOYMENT: PASS`) like its siblings, and assert the absence of a bare `GATE:` token in every `scripts/*-check.js` so a third one cannot appear. Until then, read the **LAST** `GATE:` line, never the first match | G-DNC |

Nothing in the schema forbids two `active` years per school, and nothing requires the active year to contain today — **both invariants FAIL on the data above**, so they need an expand/contract migration **and** a decision about what "active" means for the two violating rows. Recorded as **`PF-328`**, deliberately deferred; `G-MIGRATION` is untriggered by this slice **by design**. Staleness is therefore **exposed and never selected on**: every resolution carries `isStale`, `staleByDays`, `containsReferenceDate` and `activeCount`, but nothing filters on containment — on the measured data no year contains today for either tenant, so preferring containment would blank all four portals. **`ADR-070`.** **Two land-time corrections worth keeping:** the sprint's own spec was red on two cases and both reds were the SPEC's, not the code's — one asserted `not.toContain('new Date(')` against raw source, which matched **the doc comment promising there is no clock**, i.e. the module's honesty note failed it and the only way to green it would have been to delete the documentation; the other overrode a fixture's `startDate` to today while leaving the default `endDate` in the past, building a year that ends before it begins, for which the resolver's `containsReferenceDate: false` is **correct** | G-TRUTH, G-TENANT |
| PF-36 teacher count variance 43/46/48, 25/26 | V3-E03 | S-E03-7 *(**advanced** by `S-E03-4`, run 80 — NOT closed)* | `open` — **advanced: the shared substrate is canonical; the counts themselves are unmeasured** | partial — same evidence as `PF-04`; **no teacher count has been re-measured across portals since the change** | **P1 `[truth]`.** Three of the diverging figures are computed by `AnalyticsService.adminDashboard` and its drill-downs, which resolved the active academic year **three separate times, each without an `orderBy`** (`analytics.service.ts:2386,:3096,:3265` plus `school-performance-drilldown.service.ts:138`) — a plausible contributor to counts that differ per surface, now removed. **But the claim stops there, deliberately:** nobody has re-run the 43/46/48 and 25/26 measurements, so whether the variance narrowed is **unknown**, not "fixed". **Additionally relevant when this row is picked up:** the 7 `AnalyticsService.adminDashboard` specs behind exactly these figures are still red at baseline (`PF-63`, all 7 in `scripts/known-test-failures.json`), so this surface has **no passing test behind it** — closing `PF-36` almost certainly means closing `PF-63` first  **ADVANCED AGAIN by `S-E03-13` (run 103, `ADR-087`) — and still NOT closed.** A *second* divergence mechanism is removed by construction: `TeachingAssignment` carried **two** year axes — its own `academic_year_id` column and its section’s — the container database **accepts** rows where the two contradict (measured, `INSERT 0 1`, rolled back), and eight production reads filtered on the column axis. All eight now derive the year from the SECTION through one named predicate, `assignmentYearScopeWhere`. **One of the eight is `teachers.controller.ts:434`, `GET /teachers/:id/load` — the audit’s VARIANT C, the literal “43”.** Both axes were counted identical on live data *before* converting (286/286 assignments, 186/186 distinct teachers, delta 0 across all four years), so no displayed number moves today; what changes is that they can no longer *drift* apart. **The claim still stops where run 80’s did:** nobody has re-measured 43/46/48 or 25/26 across the four portals, and `PF-63` (7 red baseline specs behind exactly these figures) is still open. ~15 nested reads remain on the column axis (`PF-474`), and the database constraint that would make the drift inexpressible is `PF-473`. **ADVANCED A THIRD TIME by `S-E03-14` (run 104, `ADR-088`) — and two claims in the sentence immediately above are now WRONG, corrected here rather than repeated. (a) `PF-63` is NOT still open: it was closed at run 91 by `S-E03-6` and its seven entries left `scripts/known-test-failures.json`. This row carried a stale blocker for thirteen runs, and "closing `PF-36` means closing `PF-63` first" is spent. (b) "Nobody has re-measured across the portals" is no longer true — it was measured, by HTTP, against the running stack.** What run 104 established, all by EXECUTION: the audit's divergence MECHANISM reproduced against the container (`BEGIN … ROLLBACK` — one pupil injected into a second of the demo teacher's sections makes cumulative and distinct disagree, **58 vs 57**, the "46 cumulative / 43 distinct" shape); and `S-E03-13`'s "eight sites" claim was **incomplete** — a NINTH teacher-scoped read, `analytics.service.ts` `teacherReports` (`GET /analytics/teacher-reports`), still carried the column-axis literal, so `/teacher/reports` and `/teacher/dashboard` returned **different sets of classes to the same teacher** whenever an assignment's column contradicted its section. **The cause was structural, and it is why this slice ships a ratchet rather than a tenth test:** run 103's `assignment-year-axis.spec.ts` is per-site behavioural — a hand-kept list, which *by construction* cannot fail on the site it omitted (the run-59 « deux listes à la main » failure). It is replaced by a DERIVED one-way ratchet (`assignment-year-axis-derivation-gate.spec.ts`) whose RED control, executed against the real pre-fix source restored from the git index, names **exactly** `analytics.service.ts:4199 (findMany)` and nothing else. **Why this row STILL does not close:** the audit's literal 43/46/48 and 25/26 are **not present in today's seed and cannot be** — all 2463 enrollments are `active` and no pupil sits in two of one teacher's sections (`PF-478`) — and fabricating data to make those numbers reappear would be inventing the evidence. What is proven is that the surfaces **agree on one fixture, including under the divergent condition**. Open axes: `PF-473` (the composite FK, which removes the drift from the DATA and not merely from the NUMBERS), `PF-474` + `PF-477` (the nested reads, and this ratchet's declared reach), `PF-413` (the year-scope asymmetry between `getLoad` and `teacherDashboard`). **One warning for whoever picks this row up: the `pilotage_api` image serving `localhost:4000` was FOUR DAYS OLDER than `main` when this run began (`PF-476`), so the first probe pass measured code that had never been deployed. Rebuild before believing any live number, and record the image age beside it** | G-TRUTH |

| PF-339 ~~**P1**~~ → **FALSIFIED** *(raised by run 81 / `S-E03-2`; **settled by EXECUTION at run 99 / `S-E03-2b`**, the first run in eight with a live Postgres)* — ~~**`if (!g.value) continue` deletes a legitimate grade of ZERO from every A-backed surface, including the north-star dashboard**~~ — **it does not, and never did: `Grade.value` is `Decimal?`, Prisma returns a `Prisma.Decimal` OBJECT, and every object is truthy, so `!Decimal(0)` is `false` and the zero was KEPT.** The **latent** hazard the row's own fine print named is real, and is now immunised | V3-E03 | **`S-E03-2b`** *(run 99 — the four sites adopt the explicit predicate; the claim is falsified, not fixed)* | `falsified` — **NOT `closed`: nothing was repaired, an assertion was refuted.** Measured 2026-08-29 against `database=pilotage server=172.18.0.10` (the CONTAINER db — `localhost:5432` is the other, native-Windows one) over 420 real grades | `scripts/grade-zero-value-probe.js` — **`PROBE: PASS — 5/5`**, both polarities pinned: `P1` `!Decimal(0)===false` ⇒ **kept**; `P2` `!Number(Decimal(0))===true` ⇒ **dropped once anything numerifies**; `P3` new predicate keeps 0 in both representations and still drops `NULL`; `P4` transaction rolled back, 3/3 rows unmutated · `apps/api/src/modules/analytics/grade-zero-value.spec.ts` — **8/8 green after, 3 RED before** on the restored pre-fix predicate | **Severity moved DOWN on measurement, and the row still resolved — the `ADR-068 §1.1` move, and it is a MEASUREMENT, not a re-labelling. Do not "correct" it back upward on a re-read of the headline alone.** What made the change worth shipping anyway: the zero survived **by accident**, on an invariant *nothing in the code stated* — that the value reaches the loop unconverted — while the SAME file already does `Number(g.value)` at l. 834 and l. 1297. The file also carried **two idioms for one test**: four sites `!g.value`, four sites `g.value === null \|\| g.value === undefined`. Three of the four bad sites are inside `parentDashboard`, i.e. **projection A itself**; the fourth carries the admin `overall` KPI, where the fixture measures **50 % → 100 %** — the predicate does not degrade the KPI, it **invents** it (`DNC-01`). Closed as a **CLASS**, so a ratchet is mandatory: `grade-zero-value.spec.ts` scans `apps/api/src` + `apps/worker/src` from a **derived** `git ls-files` corpus, on **de-commented** code (`PF-366`), with three anti-vacuity controls — corpus > 100 files, fires on an injected violation, and **fires on the real production file reconstituted pre-fix** (≥ 4 hits). `ADR-084` | G-TRUTH, G-PORTAL |

Run 99 then repaired those four sites and the pin failed — correctly, but as an obstacle rather than a signal, on a file the slice never named and never ran. **The general class:** a source-pinning assertion inverts the usual dependency — the spec depends on the *text* of production code, so the better production gets, the redder the spec goes, and the coupling appears in no import graph and no test-selection heuristic. **The remedy that was applied, and is the one to repeat:** when the pinned defect is fixed, **invert the pin** (`not.toContain` + a witness that the subject still exists), which is strictly stronger than the original — it forbids the return of the idiom instead of recording its presence. **Never delete the pin to reach green**; that is the gate-weakening Step −2 forbids outright. **Cheap mitigation, not built:** the sprint's test selection reads the diff, so a grep for the changed literals across `*.spec.ts` would have named this file in seconds | G-DNC |

Les 67 sont TOUS dans `src/shared/quality/*-gate.spec.ts` (specs qui lancent des sous-processus, marchent l’arbre ou exigent un vrai PostgreSQL), et le diff de la branche touche **ZÉRO fichier sous `shared/`**. **Le delta n’est plus 11 vs 10 mais 4 vs 67 sur le MÊME arbre selon la seule charge** : la fragilité est bien pire que ce que le run 100 pouvait voir, et un ratchet qui varie d’un facteur 16 selon la machine ne peut pas distinguer une régression d’un bruit. Same pass: `AC-8` names the file `ADR-071-student-read-authorisation-is-one-service.md` while the file on disk is `ADR-071-student-read-authorisation-and-failed-reads.md`. (b) `apps/web/src/lib/read-result.ts` introduces a **third** result-type family (`ReadResult`, `ReadFailure`, `isAccessDenied`) beside `ApiResult`/`ApiFailure`, while `§D5`'s reuse table names only `ApiResult`, `apiResultFromError` and `ErrorState` — so the module 58 remaining `safe()` pages are meant to migrate onto (`PF-336`) has **no ADR entry**. It also value-imports `apiResultFromError` from `@/lib/api-client`, a **server** module whose own docblock (`:12-19`) warns that a value-import from a `'use client'` file breaks `next build` (`PF-133`); `read-result.ts` carries no such warning and sits under the generic `@/lib` exporting a predicate a client component would naturally reach for. **Fix direction:** reword `§D3` to defer the predicate to `S-E03-3`, fix the file-name reference, add a `§D5` sub-entry naming `@/lib/read-result` as canonical (and why it carries `status` when `ApiFailure` deliberately does not), and copy the server-module warning into its docblock — or move `isAccessDenied` into the leaf `@/lib/api-error-message` | G-PORTAL |
| PF-354 **P1** *(discovered at land, run 81, by the routine EXECUTING its own probe and refusing the first green it was handed)* — **an authorisation probe cannot tell « denied by the rule under test » from « denied by an unrelated rule upstream », and the first run of `parent-grades-contract-probe.js` was a FALSE GREEN** | V3-E02 *(evidence power)* | — *(the instance is CLOSED in the same diff — `assertReached()` in the probe; the CLASS is open and deliberately not storified)* | `closed` for the instance, `open` for the class — **measured 2026-08-25** by diagnosing a 403 instead of crediting it | `scripts/parent-grades-contract-probe.js` — `assertReached()`, plus the two executed runs recorded on `PF-343` | **The probe’s first execution in POST-FIX mode would have reported a perfect pass while proving nothing.** Every teacher expectation answered **403** — precisely what the fixed code should return. It was not the fixed code: `teacher@pilotage.local` had no `UserProfile` at all, so `UserSyncService.ensureUser` refused with `403 ACCOUNT_NOT_PROVISIONED` **before the request ever reached `assertCanReadStudent`**. The container was still running the pre-slice image (built 14:40, no bind mounts, verified by `docker inspect`), i.e. the fail-open was live and the probe was reporting it as fixed. What caught it was refusing to accept a green that arrived too easily and asking *which* wall produced the 403 — the same discipline `PF-127` and `PF-146` record, where a green stage was mistaken for coverage. **The instance is fixed**: `assertReached()` treats `ACCOUNT_NOT_PROVISIONED` as INCONCLUSIVE — never a pass, never a fail — and aborts naming the fixture work required. **The class is not**: every authorisation probe in `scripts/` asserts on a STATUS CODE, and `401`/`403`/`404` are each reachable from several layers (unprovisioned account, missing permission, tenant pre-check, the ABAC itself). A probe that asserts a bare status is asserting a coincidence. **Fix direction:** probes over guarded routes must assert on the DISCRIMINATING body code as well as the status, and must positively establish that the request reached the layer under test — a positive control (the same principal succeeding on a route the wall does not guard) is the cheapest form. | G-AUTHZ |

| PF-356 **P1** *(raised by run 82 / `S-E03-3` — axis 4 of the nine-axis sweep; RECORD, DON'T FIX)* — **`schoolId` asymmetry across the parent paths: the list is empty while the detail page renders fully** | V3-E03 | **`S-E03-3d`** *(run 90 — landed; **CLOSES this row**, per `ADR-076`)* | `closed` — **the list and the detail path now derive ONE population from ONE module** | `apps/api/src/modules/students/student-scope-where.spec.ts` (45 cases incl. the two-school fixture where the parent’s child is NOT in the biggest school) · `apps/api/src/shared/quality/student-school-scope-gate.spec.ts` (the one-way ratchet, 25 cases) · `apps/api/src/modules/students/students-list-teacher-scope.spec.ts` (rewired) — **70/70 executed green at land, and the ratchet executed RED (2 failed / 23 passed) against real `origin/main` sources with the controller reverted, then GREEN (25/25) restored** | **CLOSED by `S-E03-3d` (run 90), `ADR-076`.** The mechanism was NOT where this row said it was, and the correction is the finding’s real content: `student-access.service.ts:86-90` declares `scopeForUser(user, jwt, _schoolId)` with the school parameter **underscore-prefixed and genuinely unused**, so the parent ABAC scope was **already** school-agnostic and tenant-keyed. The contradiction lived one layer up, in `students.controller.ts:193-196`, which intersected that tenant-wide authority with a bare `schoolId` from `SchoolContextService.forUser` — *“the school in the tenant with the most students, ties broken by `createdAt asc`”*. So a parent whose child sat in any school but the biggest received an EMPTY list from `GET /students` while `GET /students/:id` rendered that same child in full, in the same tenant, at the same second, and `parent-digest-cron.service.ts` emailed about a child the portal denied existed. **The fix is a DISJUNCTION, not a subtraction:** `studentScopeWhere()` returns `{ tenantId, schoolId }` on the unrestricted sentinel (admins keep their deliberate working scope, proven unchanged) and `{ tenantId, id: { in: … } }` on an explicit id set — the two keys **never co-occur**, `tenantId` is on **both** branches, and `[]` still yields `id: { in: [] }`, the refusal. **Why the intersection added no security:** the id set IS the authorisation closure and is already tenant-keyed; intersecting it with a heuristic removed only LEGITIMATE children, and did so according to a value that moves when an unrelated school enrols pupils. **Corroboration the slice found and this row did not know:** `enrollments.controller.ts:678` — the SIBLING parent list — already passes `scopeForUser(me, jwt, '')` and carries no `schoolId`, so the fix makes two parent lists agree rather than inventing a rule. **Residuals recorded, not fixed:** `PF-389` (the same defect on `calendar.controller.ts` via `forTenant`), `PF-390` (`?unenrolled=true` still keyed on `forUser`’s year), `PF-394` (the dead `_schoolId` parameter at ~25 call sites), `PF-397` (`canonicalYearBySchool` now does K sequential resolutions — bound stated and asserted) | G-TRUTH, G-PORTAL |

Neither routes through `resolveActiveAcademicYear`, so both can disagree with `B1` for one child at one instant. **Fix direction:** route both through the canonical resolver (`ADR-070`) once the section-anchored family (`PF-362`) has its semantic ruling | G-TRUTH |

Both collapse to the single wire state `requested` and read the same label — but only the matched row gets the cancel button, so the parent can still read the matcher’s verdict off the UI, which is precisely what `ADR-022`’s no-oracle wall exists to prevent. **`S-E03-3b` preserved the semantics verbatim on purpose:** closing it means widening the from-status guard inside `withdraw()`, i.e. a mutation, i.e. `G-AUDIT`, i.e. its own slice. **Fix direction:** let `withdraw()` accept `match_failed` as a from-status (it revokes nothing in that case — there is no link) so the affordance stops discriminating; do NOT paper over it by hiding the button on both | G-PORTAL |

| PF-381 **P1** *(raised by run 89 / `S-E03-10` by the test-architect, who **executed** the suite rather than reading it — this is that lane's **NO-GO**)* — **the slice's own evidence is pure string algebra, it introduced two NEW red tests, and the rewritten revive path is now entirely unexercised** | V3-E03 | **`S-E03-10`** *(raised AND closed inside the same slice, at the land pass)* | **`closed` 2026-08-26 (run 89 land pass)** — **measured before**: `npx jest src/modules/analytics-snapshots/snapshot-recompute.spec.ts` in `apps/worker` = **38 tests, 7 failed**; 5 in `scripts/known-test-failures.json`, **2 new**. **Measured after**: `npx jest snapshot` in `apps/worker` = **45 tests, 40 passed, 5 failed — all five baselined, 0 excess** | `apps/worker/src/modules/analytics-snapshots/snapshot-trigger-conflict.spec.ts` (**7 cases, the mechanism evidence**) · the two repaired harnesses in `snapshot-recompute.spec.ts` | **Regression 1** — `failed-row revival (E6-S5 PM-G)`: `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'indexOf')` at `packages/contracts/src/dto/snapshot.ts:129` ← `requeueCanonical` ← `reviveFailedTriggers:273`. The harness at `:645` mocks `findMany` as `[{ id: 'f1' }, { id: 'f2' }]` with no `coalesceKey`, while the diff added `coalesceKey` to the `select`. **Stale mock, not a production NPE** (`coalesceKey String` is non-nullable) — but the consequence is that `reviveFailedTriggers`, which this diff rewrote from a batch `updateMany` into a per-row loop, is now **entirely unexercised**. **Regression 2** — `coefficient_changed fan-out (E6-S3 FR7)`: `makeDrainHarness`'s `findFirst` returns a trigger without `coalesceKey`, so `terminalCoalesceKey` throws *inside* the try, the drain falls to the failure branch, the settle throws again — and is swallowed by the **newly added** `catch (settleErr)`. The first thing that new swallow did in anger was **mask a crash and silently mark nothing done**, which is `PF-24`'s own failure shape. **The proof of insufficiency is empirical, not rhetorical:** the service is broken right now on the `done` path in that harness and **all five new tests pass** — they would stay green through a full re-introduction of `PF-24`. **Fix direction:** one service-level test on `drainTenant` driving the SECOND recompute of a scope against an in-memory fake that **enforces** `@@unique([tenantId, coalesceKey, status])`; assert the surviving row ends `done`, that its key is not `K` but `canonicalCoalesceKey(key) === K` (a bare status assertion asserts a coincidence — run 81's lesson), that the first row is untouched, **and a positive control proving the fake actually raises P2002**. Repair the two stale harnesses in the same pass and re-run to **0 excess**. **DONE AT THE LAND PASS OF THIS SAME RUN (89), exactly as prescribed** — `snapshot-trigger-conflict.spec.ts` builds an in-memory `snapshot_recompute_trigger` that **enforces** `@@unique([tenantId, coalesceKey, status])` (validating every candidate row of an `updateMany` before mutating any, so Prisma's all-or-nothing statement semantics are reproduced and the "one bad row aborts the sweep" assertions are real) and throws a `code: 'P2002'`. **The positive control is the FIRST test in the file** and asserts the fake bites, so no later assertion is vacuous. **Each case was proven RED against a re-introduced defect, not merely observed green:** removing the terminal-key mangling turns `AC-1`, `AC-1b` and the parking case red (3 of 7); removing the claim guard turns `AC-2a` red and nothing else — so the tests discriminate BETWEEN the four sites rather than covering them jointly. **`reviveFailedTriggers` is no longer unexercised** (`AC-2c` drives it with a conflicting row present and asserts the canonical key comes back) | G-TRUTH |
