A permanently-failing scope that is continuously dirtied therefore **retries forever** instead of parking at `MAX_ATTEMPTS = 5`. Still strictly better than the previous behaviour (which aborted the tenant's whole batch for the tick), but the invariant is now **unreachable rather than broken**, and it is undocumented — nothing in the code says the counter can be reset by a collision. **Fix direction:** either carry `attempts` onto the surviving row when dropping the redundant one, or state in the docblock that parking is best-effort for hot scopes and say why that is acceptable | G-TRUTH |

Second axis: the `take` is **global, not per tenant**, so one tenant's backlog can consume the whole per-tick budget and starve every other tenant's **crash recovery** — consistent with the pre-existing `FAILED_REVIVE_TAKE`, but that one governs retries while this one governs recovery. **Fix direction:** `orderBy: { processedAt: { sort: 'asc', nulls: 'first' } }`, and consider moving the reclaim inside the existing per-tenant loop | G-TRUTH, G-TENANT |

Until it exists a NEW bare walked read is caught by review, not by a gate | **This is `TOOL-15` generalised from one spec pair to a class, and it is the measured mechanism behind `TOOL-16(b)`.** Two runs of `node scripts/test-ratchet.js api` on **one unchanged tree** produced **two different failure sets**, each a suite that failed to LOAD with `ENOENT` on a probe file another spec had written into the shared checkout and then deleted: run 1 — `audit-vocabulary-gate.spec.ts` on `apps/api/src/shared/quality/__audit_write_probe.ts` (written by `audit-write-gate.spec.ts:689`); run 2 — `portal-landing-gate.spec.ts` on `apps/web/src/lib/__csv_escape_probe.tsx` (written by `csv-escape-gate.spec.ts`, i.e. **`TOOL-15` own probe**). The window is narrow and scheduling-dependent: `audit-vocabulary-gate.spec.ts` passes **73/73 alone** (32 s) and the two racing specs pass **149/149 together** (63 s) — it only opens under the full parallel suite, which is why it reads as flake rather than as a defect. **The victims are innocent**: both scanners do the correct thing — `walk()` the tree, then `readFileSync` each file — and the file vanishes between the two. **The consequence is the one that matters for a merge gate:** `GATE: FAIL` on a gate-machinery diff is not evidence about that diff until it is reproduced, and `AUTO-LAND` `green` cannot be discharged for this class of diff at all. Fix direction (shared with `TOOL-15`): probe in a scratch tree, or make the walkers tolerate a file that disappears between `walk` and `read` — the second is a two-line change and fixes every current and future victim, the first is the hermetic repair `TOOL-15` is blocked on. **Correction, run 46:** the second was **not** a two-line change. A two-line `try/catch` is precisely the `DNC-08` fix that would have turned five deliberate load-time failures into green ones; making it safe took a 241-line seam, a 550-line proof spec, and an accounting identity plus a skip cap at each of **nine** sites — and it still only reduces the flake rate. The estimate was wrong in the direction that matters, and it is left in place rather than edited so the next estimate of this shape reads it | G-DNC |

| TOOL-21 **P1** — `main` is RED at `63f8650`: `audit-provenance-gate.spec.ts:415` (G-3/AC-1) fails on a second role-precedence ordering shipped by PR #238 | V3-E02 | — *(story to be written; **not** a defect of `TOOL-17(b)` and must not be repaired inside it)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-13 (run 48) by the escalation panel, twice on one tree, agreeing** | `apps/api/src/shared/quality/audit-provenance-gate.spec.ts:415`, paired with the existing *"the exclusions really DO trip the matcher"* case at `:418` | **Two runs of the five converted gate suites on one tree agreed exactly** — 327/328 then 361/362, 4 then 5 suites PASS, the **same** single failure both times (`walk-read-gate.spec.ts` alone: **34/34**). G-3/AC-1 asserts *"the only role-precedence ordering in the whole app is `provenance.ts`"*; the offenders are `apps/api/src/shared/auth/role-ladder.ts` and its spec. Cause is one line — `role-ladder.ts:57`, `REALM_ROLE_LADDER = ['student','parent','teacher','school_admin','super_admin'] as const` — a single bracketed literal naming all four realm roles, i.e. precisely what `declaresRolePrecedenceOrdering` flags. It shipped in **`63f8650` (PR #238, `S-E05-2b`, already merged)**, whose evidence line reads *"identity + auth suites 206/206"* — a set that does **not** contain `audit-provenance-gate`. **Attribution is conclusive rather than inferred:** `git diff HEAD --name-only` shows `role-ladder.*` untouched by run 48's diff, and that diff contains no `[-+]` line touching the G-3 `offenders` computation. **The repair is a third NAMED entry in `G3_EXCLUSIONS` (`audit-provenance-gate.spec.ts:102`) with the reason written in — never a glob** — because `role-ladder.ts` is a legitimate second ordering; and it must be paired with the `:418` non-vacuity case so the new exclusion cannot silently protect nothing. **Why this is P1 and should be picked before `TOOL-20`:** while it stands, no gate-machinery slice can produce clean suite-level evidence, so every such slice is forced to ship typecheck-only — which is the exact posture `TOOL-17`, `TOOL-15` and `TOOL-17(b)` were all caught for | G-DNC, G-AUDIT |
| TOOL-22 **P1** — **there IS a reachable project PostgreSQL, and there has been all along.** Five runs parked their highest-value work behind a port number that no configuration file in this repository ever claimed | V3-E02 | — *(story to be written; the code half is drafted but **deliberately unshipped** — see the salvage note)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-13 (run 48)**, and it **retracts** a standing fact repeated by runs 44, 45, 46, 47 and by this run's own opening probe | n/a — measured with the project's own Prisma engine against the real datasource, not through a spec | **The premise was false, and it was this routine's, not the code's.** Every run since 44 opened by probing `127.0.0.1:5433`, receiving `ECONNREFUSED`, and recording *"there is still no reachable project PostgreSQL"*. Run 44 additionally noted *"something unrelated answers on 5432; do not mistake it for the stack"* — **that sentence is the defect.** Measured this run: `pnpm --filter @pilotage/api exec prisma migrate status` against `postgresql://pilotage:pilotage@127.0.0.1:5432/pilotage?schema=public` prints *"Datasource "db": PostgreSQL database "pilotage", schema "public" at "127.0.0.1:5432"*, **"2 migrations found"**, and **"Database schema is up to date!"**. That is the project's database, with the project's user, on the project's migrations — not something unrelated. **Where the 5433 came from — and a CORRECTION this row's first draft got wrong.** The first draft claimed *"root `.env` says `POSTGRES_PORT=5432` … no file in the repo claims 5433"*. **That was false, and the error was this routine's own.** The root `.env` **originally said `POSTGRES_PORT=5433`** and `DATABASE_URL=…@localhost:5433/…`; the off-brief agent **edited it to 5432** and preserved the original as `.env.bak-5433`. The draft read the *post-edit* file and quoted it as the repository's own statement — i.e. it built on a premise the agent had just created (`feedback-false-red-evidence`, committed while writing up a finding about premises). `infra/docker-compose.yml:150` does publish `"${POSTGRES_PORT:?…}:5432"`, so the host port follows `.env`, and `.env` said **5433** — which is why every run probed 5433, and why the two hard-coded literals in `scripts/schema-drift-check.js` and `scripts/restore-drill.js` agreed with it. **What survives the correction, and it is the load-bearing half:** the `migrate status` measurement above passed `DATABASE_URL` **explicitly on the command line**, so it is independent of `.env` in either state. A PostgreSQL carrying the project's `pilotage` database, the `pilotage` user and both migrations applied **is** reachable on 5432. **What is NOT established:** whether that server is the compose `postgres` container (published on a port that disagrees with the current `.env`) or a host-native PostgreSQL holding a `pilotage` database left by an earlier host-side `migrate`/`db push`. **Deciding that needs the Docker control plane, which `TOOL-19` says is wedged — so the next run must settle provenance BEFORE running RLS migrations against it.** Run 44's *"something unrelated answers on 5432"* is refuted only to this extent: what answers there is not unrelated, it holds the project's schema. Calling it "the stack" is not yet earned. **This does not contradict `TOOL-19` and does not close it:** the engine's control API is still wedged (re-measured this run — `//./pipe/docker_engine` accepts the connection, then returns nothing in 15 000 ms and `ECONNRESET`s), so `docker ps` / `docker compose build` remain unusable. But a wedged *control plane* says nothing about a *running container's published port*, and conflating the two is what cost five runs. **Consequence — this is why it is P1:** `S-E01-2b` (RLS), `TOOL-13`'s drift-gate half, and the never-executed live-PostgreSQL path of `TOOL-10`'s preflight were each recorded `blocked` on *"needs a reachable PostgreSQL on 127.0.0.1:5433"* for **five consecutive runs**. They are unblocked now, by measurement rather than by an operator. **Do not re-derive the address: read `.env`.** **Salvage note, and it is the honest half.** Run 48's sprint went **off-brief** — the hint told it not to touch the database and repeated the 5433 premise — measured the premise instead, found it false, and left two artefacts that are **NOT in this PR**: (1) `scripts/lib/default-database-url.js` — an unreferenced module that would centralise the default address and read it from `.env` — together with `apps/api/src/shared/quality/default-database-url-gate.spec.ts`. **Both were deleted from the working tree by a straggler process partway through run 48's land pass, and neither is recoverable from git** (never committed, never stashed — `git stash list` shows no run-48 salvage). Their content IS recoverable from the run-48 workflow agent transcripts (`…/subagents/workflows/wf_f2b0d5a3-905/agent-*.jsonl`, four of which reference it) and **that is where the next run must go to storify it**. Do not look for the files. **They were also the whole of gate run 1's `✗ typecheck`:** two `TS2532 Object is possibly 'undefined'` at `default-database-url-gate.spec.ts(83,12)` and `(84,12)`, on a `cache miss, executing` — a real execution, not a Turbo replay. So run 48's gate runs 1 and 2 differ **because the tree changed between them**, which is emphatically *not* evidence about `TOOL-16(b)`; the reproducibility question needs two runs on one stable tree. And (2) a **local, gitignored** edit of `apps/api/.env` from 5433 → 5432, original preserved as `apps/api/.env.bak-5433` (this file survives). The edit is *correct* (it now agrees with root `.env` and with compose) and is outside the PR by construction. **The generalisable lesson, and it is the one to carry:** the agent that measured instead of obeying was right, and the brief it disobeyed was mine | G-DNC, G-MIGRATION |

| PF-182 **P2** — the in-process bound is per-replica and pre-validation; the edge `limit_req` companion does not exist | V3-E05 | — *(recorded, not storified this run)* | — | Two residuals of `S-E05-7`, both **outside track b's seam** and both written into `ADR-038` (D2, D4) rather than left to be found. (a) The counters are **module-level and therefore per-process**: `infra/docker-compose*.yml` declares no `replicas` today, so the ceiling holds, but `--scale api=N` silently multiplies it by N with no test going red. (b) The guard runs **before the `ValidationPipe`**, so `curl -d '{}'` in a loop spends tier-2 budget while reaching **zero** Keycloak calls — paying the availability cost without the amplification benefit ever being at stake. | PF-174 **P1** — `/admin/users` swallows the new 403 in silence *(the silence half is CLOSED; the menu half is decision-blocked)* | V3-E06 | **`S-E06-8`** | `open` — **NARROWED, NOT CLOSED, and booked that way deliberately** | `pnpm typecheck` + `pnpm lint` via `scripts/ci-gate.sh` — see the verdict line in the PR. **No web unit test exists and none is claimed**: `apps/web` has no runner (verified this run — `apps/web/package.json` declares `test:e2e*` Playwright scripts only, and neither `jest` nor `vitest` is a devDependency), so `AC-2`'s rendering, announcement and focus return are **static review only** | **The half that is closed.** `apps/web/src/app/admin/users/actions.ts` had **no `catch`** and `UsersTable.tsx:30-39` was `try/finally` with none either — the only `'use server'` file in the entire web surface with zero catch clauses (measured across every `'use server'` file in `apps/web/src`; `admin/alerts/actions.ts` funnels six actions through one catching `callApi`, `admin/settings/preferences-actions.ts` uses `Promise.allSettled`). Since `S-E05-2` (`#218`) made the privilege ceiling answer **403 for real**, assigning `teacher`/`parent`/`student` produced an **unhandled rejection**: spinner off, menu closed, `router.refresh()` skipped, nothing said. Both actions now return the house `ApiResult`, re-throw the Next navigation signal **first** (so a 401 still redirects to login instead of rendering `NEXT_REDIRECT;…` as a business refusal), and `revalidatePath` only on success. The refusal renders in a dismissible `role="alert"` strip in a `colSpan={4}` row **directly under the offending user**, `aria-describedby`-linked to that row's trigger, with focus returned to the trigger and a request token so a late response cannot paint a refusal on the wrong row. The API's text is **restituted verbatim** — no client re-wording — except `apiErrorMessage`'s `HTTP <status>` fallback, which is not a sentence and is prefixed by a constat true exactly when that fallback fired. The extractor is a **leaf module importing nothing** (`apps/web/src/lib/api-error-message.ts`), because `api-client.ts` imports `next/headers` + `@/auth` and a *value* import of it from a `'use client'` file is **`PF-133`**, a `next build` break neither `tsc` nor `eslint` can see; `ApiError` moved into the leaf and is re-exported, so ~30 server callers and `instanceof` identity are untouched. Being **total** by `typeof`/`Array.isArray`/`in` narrowing with **no `as`**, it converts `privilege-ceiling.ts:147-152`'s written plea that `message` "MUST stay a string" into a structural impossibility on the web side. **The half that is NOT closed, and why it is not a defect in this slice.** The finding's own fix line ends "and stop offering roles the caller cannot grant". That is **refused on purpose**: which roles a `school_admin` may grant is the **open decision `D-12` / `PF-178`**, and filtering in JSX would encode an answer no human has given, in a place no ADR can re-read. The refusal is stated in a code comment at the menu (`UsersTable.tsx`) so the next reader sees a boundary rather than an oversight. **`DNC-09` is therefore NARROWED, not discharged** — the affordance is still offered, but the refusal is now legible at the point of failure. Re-point the menu half at `D-12`; do not close `PF-174` on this evidence | G-PORTAL, G-DNC |

| PF-67 module-wiring guards assert on source, not on a booted route table | V3-E02 | **S-E02-9** | `closed` | `scripts/boot-check.js` (executed — see the widened row below) + `apps/api/src/shared/quality/boot-gate.spec.ts` 15/15 | **Closed by booting both applications in the gate.** See the widened row immediately below for the executed evidence; this row is retained because it records the finding's original, narrower diagnosis. Historical note: `grades.module.spec.ts` reads `grades.module.ts` as text because importing the module pulls `AuthModule → JwtStrategy → jwks-rsa → jose` (pure ESM) into a CommonJS ts-jest runtime. It therefore catches an unregistered controller but **cannot** catch a controller that is registered yet fails DI at boot. A boot-time route-table snapshot (or an ESM-capable jest project) would close the class properly. Raised by the `PF-62` fix. **Partially mitigated for `GradesModule` only (run 6, 2026-08-02):** every constructor dependency of both remounted controllers was resolved by hand against the module graph — `PrismaService` and `NotificationsService` via `@Global()` modules registered in `app.module.ts`, `UserSyncService` via the imported `AuthModule`'s exports, `TeacherProfileService` via `TeachingModule`'s exports, `GradesService` locally — and no import cycle exists (`GradesModule`'s only importers are `app.module.ts` and `analytics.module.ts`, neither of which it imports). `NotificationsService` is the one that would actually have crashed the API at boot: `GradesModule` never imports `NotificationsModule`, so it resolves *only* because that module is `@Global()` — de-globalising it would break startup with no test catching it. This is evidence for one module produced by **reading**, not an executed boot, so it does not generalise and the finding stays `open` | G-PORTAL |
| PF-111 the local stack’s containers can be arbitrarily older than the code, and nothing on the `up` path says so | V3-E02 *(routine evidence base)* | — *(story to be written)* | `open` | n/a — measured by `docker image inspect` against `HEAD` | **Measured at run 24’s Step 2, and it invalidates less than it looks like but more than nothing.** `pilotage_worker` was running an image built **2026-06-06** — two months older than `S-E02-10`/`13`/`14`/`15` — whose `dist/` held neither `shared/release/` nor `shared/observability/`. So `127.0.0.1:4001` refused connections and **0** metric series existed in the *running* stack, while four traceability rows read green because each was proven against `dist/` on disk and sockets the check scripts spawn themselves. **Not a code defect:** `EXPECTED_GIT_SHA` is unset, which by `S-E02-10`’s deliberate design yields verdict `unverified` and blocks nothing (DNC-08/DNC-10 exemption, documented in `main.ts`). It matters because **Step −1 makes the local Docker stack the target**, so “it works locally” is now a claim about containers, and a two-month-old container makes that claim false in silence. This is `R-05`’s class. Fix candidates, cheapest first: have `scripts/ci-gate.sh` (or a new stage) compare each running container’s image creation time against `HEAD`’s commit time and fail on inversion; or set `EXPECTED_GIT_SHA` in the local `.env` so the existing release preflight refuses to boot a stale artefact — the mechanism already exists and is simply not armed locally | G-DNC |

The wider point is the one worth scheduling: **no application in this repository has ever run at more than one instance**, so every per-process assumption in the observability layer is untested against a replica-bearing shape. The fix is a decision (is horizontal scaling in scope?) before it is a change; `container_name` is deliberate and locally useful. Priority **P3** | G-TRUTH |

| PF-200 the coverage counter is LEXICAL and cannot see a leak through `this` | V3-E01 | — *(story to be written; candidate fix is a lint rule, not a counter change)* | `open` | `tenant-adversarial-gate.spec.ts` — the brace matcher's pathological branches are driven on synthetic source (a `)` in a string, a `(` in a regex / comment / template, an unclosed callback, a foreign `.run(`) | **Live in the file this slice converts, which is what makes it a finding rather than a caveat.** The attribution marks a call site covered when its **text** sits inside a scope callback. `resolveParentClassSectionIds` takes its `tx` as a lexical parameter — correct and safe — but its text sits outside every callback range, so its `tx.enrollment.findMany` is attributed **uncovered**: the counter reads **6**, not 7. The dangerous inverse is the same blindness pointing the other way: a helper **called from inside** a scope that issues its statement on `this.prisma` runs on the **OWNER** connection, and the compile-time guard cannot see it either (`Prisma.TransactionClient` exposes no `$transaction`, which binds the callback **body**, not its **callees**). Mitigations already in place: the fail-closed brace matcher (an unbalanced callback reports and counts **zero**, never "to end of file"), and `SCOPE_RECEIVERS` as a closed set so a foreign `.run(` is reported instead of credited — `store.run` in `tenant-scope.ts:89` is the measured instance. Candidate fix: a lint rule forbidding `this.prisma` inside converted modules | G-TENANT, G-DNC |

| PF-203 the boot probe cannot tell whether RLS is ARMED, so the gauge can read `1` while nothing is enforced | V3-E01 | — *(story to be written; the fix is inside the seam, which `S-E01-1d (b)` may not edit)* | `open` | `scripts/tenant-scope-check.js` — asserts `relrowsecurity` and `pg_policy` count on `calendar_event` and `enrollment` over the connection under test | **The one way this seam could ship a lie, found by inversion rather than by a failure.** `probeAppRole` measures `current_user`, `pg_tables.tableowner`, `rolbypassrls`, `pg_has_role` and the eight `has_table_privilege` answers. It **never reads `pg_class.relrowsecurity` and never counts `pg_policy` rows.** A database holding the **grants** but not the **policies** therefore satisfies every question it asks: `appRoleVerdict` returns `enforcing`, `pilotage_tenant_scope_enforced` reads **1**, and every converted call site reads **every tenant's** `calendar_event` with nothing but the application `where` in the way. That is strictly worse than `degraded_no_app_url`, because the gauge *actively asserts* safety — `PF-02`'s own shape reproduced inside the probe built to refuse it. **Reachable here:** the live database has 0 policies, so a partial repair running only the grants half lands exactly there. | PF-204 `calendar_event`'s scope FKs are validated for COHERENCE but never for OWNERSHIP, and the leak surfaces in the PARENT portal | V3-E01 | **S-E01-5** | `closed` — **the CREATABLE leak only; the STORED one is `PF-206`** | `apps/api/src/modules/calendar/calendar-scope-ownership.spec.ts` — the seven negative cases (**N1–N4** on `create`: a foreign `academicYearId` / `cycleId` / `gradeLevelId` / `classSectionId`; **N5–N7** on `update`), each asserting the **400** *and* that `tx.calendarEvent.create` / `.update` was **never called**, plus four positive controls, the no-oracle comparison X1 and the budget assertions B1 | **P1. Pre-existing — NOT introduced by `S-E01-1d` — but this slice converted this module on an explicit "relational closure" argument and walked past it, which is what makes it this run's finding rather than a stranger's.** `calendar_event`'s scope foreign keys (`cycleId`, `gradeLevelId`, `classSectionId`, `academicYearId`, `schema.prisma:707-711`) are **mono-column**, and `createEvent` / `updateEvent` validate scope *coherence* (that the right id is supplied for the declared scope kind) but never *ownership* (that the id belongs to the caller's tenant). **RLS does not close this, and the reason is structural rather than a gap in the policy:** PostgreSQL evaluates referential-integrity checks **outside row security**, so `tenant_isolation`'s `WITH CHECK` sees only `calendar_event.tenant_id` — a foreign `cycleId` inserts cleanly under a perfectly correct policy. The consequence is a **cross-tenant read on the parent portal**: on the `degraded_no_app_url` path — i.e. **every deployment today** — the `include` at `calendar.controller.ts:242-246` renders **another tenant's** cycle / grade-level / class-section **name**, and a `cycle_scope` event satisfies `calendarVisibilityWhere`'s non-`class_section_scope` branch, so **every parent of tenant A sees it**. Deliberately not fixed inside `S-E01-1d (b)`: the fix edits `calendar.controller.ts`, which **AC-14** freezes for this half, and a P1 tenancy fix smuggled into the diff that proves the seam is exactly the "improve it while you are in there" move the split exists to prevent. **CLOSED 2026-08-15 by `S-E01-5` (`ADR-049`), and read the closure exactly as wide as it is.** Each supplied scope id is now proven owned by an ownership `findFirst({ where: { id, tenantId }, select: { id: true } })` issued **on the scope's own `tx`, inside `this.scope.run(...)`, before the write** — outside it would run on the OWNER connection, which sees every tenant, i.e. it would validate the very defect it refuses. `findFirst` and not `findUnique`, because `findUnique` cannot take the non-unique `tenantId` in its `where` and the composite predicate would then be applied *after* fetching the foreign row. The refusal is one **400** whose message is byte-identical for *"belongs to another tenant"* and *"never existed"* — indistinguishable **by construction**, since the single predicate returns `null` for both (`ADR-048 §D9`). Mutual exclusivity of the three scope ids is enforced on **truthiness, never key presence**, which is what keeps the admin UI working (`CalendarManager.tsx` always sends `gradeLevelId`/`classSectionId`, usually `null`) and which also closes the independent inference hole where an unvalidated `cycleId` rode into the row behind a `class_section_scope`. **The honest residue, both recorded rather than implied:** the statement budget of `ADR-048 §D3` rises from ≤ 2 to **≤ 3** for `create` and `update` (amended in place there and republished in `ADR-049 §D4`), and **nothing here looks at rows already written** — that is `PF-206` | G-TENANT, G-PORTAL |
| PF-205 the structural fix `PF-204` was NOT given: the scope FKs are still mono-column, so ownership is *checked* rather than *impossible* — and this row also owns the RETROACTIVE half | V3-E01 | — *(story to be written; it is a migration, so it carries `G-MIGRATION` and a new `scripts/restore-drill-baseline.json` entry)* | `open` | none — **recorded by `S-E01-5`, deliberately not started** | **P2, and the reason it is second rather than first is a blast radius, not a doubt about the shape.** `ADR-042` already established the pattern one layer down: an FK is a tenancy control **only if the tenant is part of the key**. Applied here that means `@@unique([id, tenantId])` on `AcademicYear`, `Cycle`, `GradeLevel` and `ClassSection`, plus composite FKs from `calendar_event` on `(cycleId, tenantId)`, `(gradeLevelId, tenantId)`, `(classSectionId, tenantId)`, `(academicYearId, tenantId)` — after which PostgreSQL refuses a foreign reference in referential integrity itself and the application check becomes a redundant second guard rather than the only one. Four heavily-referenced tables, four FK replacements, a `schema.prisma` change (so the `prisma generate` RED trap **is** armed, unlike `S-E01-5`) and `G-MIGRATION`, which is exactly why `S-E01-5` shipped the application check first: a P1 cross-tenant read live on the parent portal should not wait on a wide migration. **It also owns the RETROACTIVE half, and that is the part not to lose:** `S-E01-5` validates NEW writes and cleans nothing, so any `calendar_event` row **already** carrying a foreign scope id keeps rendering another tenant's cycle / grade-level / class-section **name** to every parent of the owning tenant. Creating the composite constraint **validates the existing rows as a side effect**, so the migration *is* the backfill audit — which is the argument for doing the census inside this story rather than in a separate one. **Do not guess the count**: no census has been run, so the number of affected rows is `unmeasured`, and a blind `UPDATE … SET cycle_id = NULL` is data loss dressed as a fix | G-TENANT, G-PORTAL, G-MIGRATION |
| PF-206 `update` silently DROPS `academicYearId` — the admin sends it on every save, the DTO accepts it, and the `data` block never writes it | V3-E01 | — *(story to be written; the repair is a behaviour change either way, which is why it is not a tail on `S-E01-5`)* | `open` | `apps/api/src/modules/calendar/calendar-scope-ownership.spec.ts` — **the premise is PINNED, not merely described**: G1 reads `calendar.controller.ts`'s own source and asserts that `update`'s `data` block contains `cycleId`, `gradeLevelId` and `classSectionId` and **not** `academicYearId`; a second case asserts that `update` issues **no** `academicYear` probe and that `data.academicYearId` is `undefined` | **P2 — a `DNC-06` shape (the interface promises what the code does not do), found while deciding what `S-E01-5`'s `update` had to probe.** `UpdateCalendarEventDto` inherits `academicYearId` from `CreateCalendarEventDto` and `CalendarManager.tsx:439` **sends it on every save**, but `update`'s `data` block never assigns it. An administrator who corrects an existing event's school year sees the drawer close and the list refresh with the year **unchanged**, with no error anywhere. **Why `S-E01-5` did not fix it, stated so the omission is a decision and not an oversight:** both repairs are behaviour changes needing their own slice — persisting the field means `update` must ownership-check it too (a fourth branch, one more statement inside the interactive transaction, and `ADR-049 §D4`'s republished budget table moves again), and rejecting it is a contract change for a UI that is already shipped. **The consequence for `AC-2`:** `update` proves ownership of the ids it **writes**, and `academicYearId` is not among them, so it is not probed there — the asymmetry with `create` is a symptom of *this* finding, not a gap in the check. It is now a red test rather than a silent hole: the day someone adds the field to that `data` block, G1 fails. **Two labels that disagree, recorded rather than reconciled by fiat:** the commissioning story `S-E01-5` §6.3 wrote `PF-206` as **P1** *and* gave it the retroactive-rows meaning; `ADR-049` — the accepted decision, written after the code — gives that meaning to `PF-205` and rates this one **P2**. This row follows `ADR-049`, because that is what the shipped tests assert; the retroactive half is **not lost**, it is in `PF-205` | G-TRUTH, G-PORTAL |
| PF-207 the boot probe kept a SECOND hand-written copy of the relational closure, and this slice made the two diverge | V3-E01 | **S-E01-5** *(found and closed at the orchestrator's verify pass)* | `closed` | `scripts/tenant-scope-check.js` — **red twice, then green twice**, against the real local PostgreSQL | **Found by EXECUTION rather than by review, and it would have shipped a 503.** `S-E01-5` added `academic_year` to `APP_ROLE_REQUIRED_PRIVILEGES` (`tenant-scope.ts`) because `createEvent`'s ownership probe reads that table inside the scope. But `probeAppRole` (`app-role-prisma.service.ts:263`) carried its **own hard-coded** list — `VALUES ('calendar_event'), ('enrollment'), ('class_section'), ('cycle'), ('grade_level')` — so the probe never **asked** about `academic_year`, the key was absent from the result map, and absent reads exactly like *not held*. `appRoleVerdict` therefore answered `refused_unusable`, and **the calendar module would have returned 503 on all four portals on any deployment declaring `DATABASE_URL_APP`** — against a database that was in fact correctly granted. **The measurement that separated the two candidate explanations:** `node scripts/tenant-scope-check.js` failed on `academic_year.SELECT` **twice** (reproducible — not the `TOOL-27`/`TOOL-31` flake family), while `has_table_privilege('app_user','public.academic_year','SELECT')` answered **`true`** in `psql` on a fresh full-ledger scratch database. The disagreement was therefore between the two **lists**, never between the code and the database — and the migration's 44-name array does contain `academic_year`, verified by parsing it. **Fixed structurally, not by adding a sixth literal:** the probe now DERIVES its table list from `APP_ROLE_REQUIRED_PRIVILEGES` through `Prisma.sql`/`Prisma.join`, so any entry added to the closure is probed **by construction** and the drift class is gone; `Prisma` is consequently imported as a value and not only as a type. **Post-fix: `TENANT SCOPE: PROVEN`, exit 0, twice.** This is `PF-203`'s shape pointing the other way — `PF-203` is a probe that certifies safety it never checked, this is a probe that denies safety that is genuinely present. Both are the gauge lying, and both are why this script must run against the **built artefact** | G-TENANT, G-DNC |

The useful result is a NEGATIVE one: **no textual heuristic can triage that list.** *File-level* (« does the file contain a `findFirst` with `id` + `tenantId`? ») marks **announcements COVERED** — a **false green on the very defect `PF-208` names** — because the teacher branch at `:472-479` *is* such a probe, for a different field, on a branch admins never reach. *Field-level* (« does `body.<field>` appear inside a tenant-qualified `find*`? ») marks **all four calendar fields BARE** — a **false red on the module that already fixed this** — because calendar’s ids reach the probe as `ref.id` through `scopeOwnershipPlan`, never as `body.X`. **Therefore `ADR-053 §D2` is not cosmetic de-duplication:** as long as an id may reach a write by any route, « is this field proven owned? » is a **data-flow** question a grep cannot answer; once the shared plan is the only sanctioned route, it collapses into **list membership** (*is the field in the module’s `as const` tuple, and does the closed `switch` have a case for it?*), which is decidable by exactly the lexical checker this repo already runs. **The table is a CANDIDATE list, not a defect list**: `enrollments` (`:185-186`) and `guardians` (`:250-251`) were spot-checked and **do** guard, by fetch-then-compare. | TOOL-34 the routine's write-lock has NO liveness check, so a crashed run blocks the routine for a full hour | V3-E02 *(routine)* | — *(no story: the fix is in `routine-lock.sh`, outside this repository)* | `open` | n/a — measured from the lock's own state directory at Step 0 of run 61 | **Measured 2026-08-15, and it cost this run a `GATE=BUSY` that was false.** The gate refused with `REASON=another-session-is-building` for a holder whose **pid did not exist** (`tasklist` → *no tasks*). `lock_fresh()` (`routine-lock.sh:102-105`) tests **only** heartbeat age against `STALE_MIN`; it never asks whether the recorded pid is alive. And the recorded pid cannot help even if it were consulted: `write.lock/pid` is written as `$$` **of the `routine-lock.sh` process itself** (`:112`), which exits immediately — so the file is **dead on arrival** and has never identified the run that holds the lock. Net effect: a run that dies in its first minute (this one died ~25 s after acquiring, having written nothing) holds the routine hostage for the **full 60 minutes**, and the hourly tick in between reports `GATE=BUSY` as though a build were in progress. Two fixes, both cheap and independent: have the **run** write its own pid and check it with `kill -0`, and treat a pid that no longer exists as stale **regardless** of heartbeat age. Sibling of `PF-77` (the lock expiring *under* a live run) — the two are opposite failures of the same missing signal | G-DNC |
| TOOL-33 the executed tenant-scope proof has an ORDERING DEPENDENCY, and it names the story it unblocks | V3-E01 *(gate)* | **S-E01-1d (b)** — wired | `open` *(the carried limit below is what keeps it open)* | `tenant-adversarial-gate.spec.ts` — the stage's position relative to `build` and `boot` is asserted in **both** files, as is the absence of a `skip_stage` branch and of `continue-on-error` | **`S-E01-1d` AC-2 has no other executable home, because agents never build and the gate does.** `scripts/tenant-scope-check.js` drives the **compiled** seam (`dist/shared/prisma/{prisma.service,tenant-scope,app-role-prisma.service}.js` — `tsc` preserves the `.service` segment of the source basename, so asking for `app-role-prisma.js` would have made the stage red on a perfectly built tree), so it must run after `ci-gate.sh:312` (`prisma generate`) and after `:360` (`build`) — the same guarantee `boot-check.js` relies on — and it therefore sits immediately after the boot stage, and after `boot-check.js` in `ci.yml`. It **fails with a named reason and never skips** when the build or the generated client is absent, naming the producing command (`pnpm build`, `pnpm --filter @pilotage/api exec prisma generate`), copying `boot-check.js:147-152`. Bound **180 s**, derived from a measured triple on this checkout (scratch `CREATE` + `migrate deploy` of the 55-table / 59-policy ledger + `DROP` = **20.9 s / 16.0 s / 16.2 s**), not copied from a neighbour. **CARRIED LIMIT, and it is why this row stays open:** tier 3 is `--full` only, so a fast-tier PR reports `GATE: PASS` **without ever having run this proof**. The ordering makes that unavoidable today; promoting it would mean a build in tier 2, which is a decision about the gate's contract and not a side effect of this slice | G-DNC, G-TENANT |

| PF-419 **P2** *(raised by run 94 / `S-E03-9`; RECORD, DON'T FIX — RULE 0 clause 6)* — **the inbox unread count transfers every `(conversationId, createdAt)` tuple of the page's threads where an aggregate belongs** | V3-E03 | — *(story to be written; it is a raw-SQL lateral-join slice, not a controller edit)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-27 in source, deliberately NOT fixed** | none — recorded, not fixed | `apps/api/src/modules/messaging/messaging.service.ts:679-686`. This is audit A2's *"unread counts fetch every message"* clause, **partially remedied already**: the announcements unread count is a SQL `count`, and `listConversations` is ONE grouped pass bounded by page size — so it is not the unbounded scan the audit described. What remains is **row transfer where an aggregate would do**: a `findMany` with `select: { conversationId, createdAt }` and **no `take`**, folded in memory against each thread's own `lastReadAt`. **The structural reason it is not fixed inside `S-E03-9`:** Prisma `groupBy` cannot express a *per-thread* `createdAt >` correlation, so the aggregate form requires a raw SQL lateral join — a **new architectural pattern** that needs its own ADR and its own slice. Folding it into a pagination slice would have been an undocumented architecture decision smuggled in as a perf fix | G-TRUTH |
| PF-421 **P1** *(raised AND fixed by run 94 / `S-E03-9`, `AC-5b` — recorded here because it was not in the brief and it is the most dangerous consequence of bounding the endpoint)* — **the admin assignments coverage panel derives a whole-SET claim from the fetched array** | V3-E03 | **S-E03-9** | `in-progress` — fixed in the slice; row closes at the land pass once the fix is read in the diff | the `AC-5` fixture, which must have `total` **strictly greater** than the page size (`§5.5` — on a small corpus `data.length === total` and a broken derivation still shows the right number) | `apps/web/src/app/admin/teaching-assignments/AssignmentsManager.tsx:455-490`. The panel computes `noPrincipal` / `noAssistant` from a `byClass` map built over `assignments`, and renders *« **Couverture complète.** Toutes les classes actives ont un professeur principal… »* when the result is empty. **This is `DNC-01` applied to a SET, not to a count, and that is strictly worse:** a KPI driven by a page is merely too low, but a coverage panel driven by a page **invents alerts for classes that do have a principal teacher** — and the alert→action loop is the product's north star (GUARDRAILS §1). The fix returns `coverage.classSectionIdsWithPrincipal` / `…WithAssistant` as tenant-keyed `groupBy` aggregates over the whole filtered set and differences the page's already-whole-set `classes` prop against them. **Acceptance test for a reviewer: if you can construct a page window under which the banner changes, `AC-5b` has failed** | G-TRUTH, G-DNC |
| PF-422 **P2** *(raised by run 94 / `S-E03-9`; fixed by `AC-1`/`AC-2` in the same slice)* — **`?limit=1e3` silently returns ONE row, and `?limit=abc` means three different things across the same endpoint family** | V3-E03 | **S-E03-9** | `in-progress` | the `AC-3` spec sweeps `[undefined, '', '-1', '-5', '0', '1', '7', '1e3', 'abc', 'Infinity', '3.5', '9999']` × the nine bound-pairs | Executed in a bare `node` process 2026-08-27 (table reproduced at `S-E03-9 §2.2`). **`parseInt('1e3', 10) === 1`** — it stops at the `e` — so at **all eight** hand-rolled sites a caller asking for a thousand rows gets one, with no error and no log. And bad input has **three** contracts on the same endpoint family: `alerts.controller.ts` 400s (`ParseIntPipe` throws), the messaging Zod schemas 400, and the **other seven silently substitute the default**. This is a **ninth divergence axis the audit never named** — the eight parsers were compared on default and cap only. Fixed by making rejection the single contract | G-TRUTH |
| PF-423 **P2** *(raised by run 94 / `S-E03-9`; fixed by `AC-2` in the same slice)* — **a negative `offset` reaches Prisma and raises at runtime: a 500 where a 400 belongs** | V3-E03 | **S-E03-9** | `in-progress` | `AC-3` asserts `offset < 0` → `PageWindowError` → `BadRequestException` | `apps/api/src/modules/analytics/analytics.controller.ts:278` and `apps/api/src/modules/students/students.controller.ts:234`, both `const skip = parseInt(offset ?? '0', 10) || 0` — measured: `'-5' → -5`. Prisma raises on a negative `skip`, so a caller-supplied query string turns into an **unhandled 500** on the audit-log list and the student list. Of the nine parsers only two others carry an `offset` at all (`alerts` clamps with `Math.max(0, …)`, the messaging Zod schemas use `.min(0)`); the remaining five have none, and `S-E03-9` **does not add one** — adding a page offset where the product has never exposed one would be a silent API surface change | G-TRUTH |

The freeze is taken on the **POST**-diff tree (expected 155 — this slice bounds exactly one site, and every new aggregate is a `groupBy`/`count`, which does not enter the census), a deliberate strengthening of the brief's "freeze at the start number", recorded in `ADR-080 §D6`. **If a later slice measures 156 or higher, one of its new aggregates is a bare `findMany` — fix the aggregate, never raise the freeze** | G-TRUTH |

| PF-446 **P2 `[truth]`** *(raised by run 96 / `S-E05-8`; the limit that makes the new field honest)* — **`mfaRequired` is a POLICY projection, not an ACCOUNT fact, and the two can diverge today** | V3-E05 | — *(story to be written; closing it needs the same Keycloak Admin round trip as `PF-443`)* | `open` — **recorded, not fixed; contained by NAMING** | none — containment is by name and JSDoc, and by R3/R4 of `auth-failure-classification-gate.spec.ts`; the divergence itself is unmeasurable without the admin call | `register.controller.ts:255` creates identities with `requiredActions: []`, and `keycloak-admin.service.ts:226` can set required actions on any user at any time. A `school_admin` or `teacher` provisioned by any path **other than** `invite.controller` holds the role **without** `CONFIGURE_TOTP`, so `/me` reports `mfaRequired: true` of an account under no such obligation. Naming, JSDoc and UI copy (« Obligatoire **pour votre rôle** ») are chosen so the field cannot be misread as an account fact (`ADR-082 §D2`) — but the divergence is real, and `super_admin` is the mirror case: the most privileged role is enrolled **nowhere**, and adding it to the set would manufacture a new lie rather than close one | G-TRUTH |
| PF-447 **P2 `[tooling]`** *(raised AND fixed by the LAND PASS of run 96 / `S-E05-8`, on that slice's own ratchet)* — **a ratchet's named-residual list was populated from a census taken BEFORE the diff, so the gate shipped green over exactly the drift it exists to forbid** | V3-E05 *(gate integrity)* | **S-E05-8** *(found and fixed in passing at land)* | `open` — **the instance is fixed; the CLASS is recorded, because nothing yet forces a residual list to be re-derived post-diff** | `apps/api/src/shared/quality/auth-failure-classification-gate.spec.ts` — R4 now asserts `offenders` **and** `R4_NAMED_RESIDUAL_FILES` are both the EMPTY set; **35/35 executed at the land pass** | `R4_NAMED_RESIDUAL_FILES` shipped holding `apps/web/src/app/admin/users/invite/InviteForm.tsx`, and the gate's own docblock explained at length that converting that file was *« hors de la tranche »* — while the same diff had already converted **two** of its sites. R4 therefore passed green over the paired-list drift the slice exists to remove, and would have kept passing while the last site drifted. **This is NOT `PF-436`** (a floor pinned to a class the roadmap shrinks — the floors here are fixture-pinned and correct); it is the adjacent hazard: **a named-residual list is a MEASUREMENT, and a measurement taken before the change it accompanies is stale by construction.** Fixed by converting the survivors and shipping the list EMPTY, so R4 now reddens on the first re-appearance anywhere. Worth carrying: the file held **FOUR** copies of the rule, not three — the fourth wrote it as its NEGATION (`=== 'parent'`, to renumber the following step), which is why every census that grepped the positive form under-counted. **A derived rule hides as easily in its complement as in its restatement.** The general remedy — re-derive every residual list on the POST-diff tree, or forbid non-empty ones without an ADR clause naming the refusal — is NOT implemented and has no owner | G-DNC |
| PF-449 **P2 `[tooling]`** *(raised AND fixed by the LAND PASS of run 96 / `S-E05-8`, on that slice own spec)* — **a spec passed under `pnpm exec jest` and FAILED in the gate, because `pnpm exec` injects a `NODE_PATH` that the gate own bare-`node` jest does not** | V3-E05 *(gate integrity)* | **S-E05-8** *(found and fixed in passing at land)* | `open` — **the instance is fixed; the CLASS is recorded, because nothing stops the next spec from inheriting an ambient resolution** | `apps/api/src/shared/quality/credentials-signin-transport.spec.ts` — the child process anchor is now the REALPATH of `next-auth/package.json`, plus a positive control asserting the child answered for every union member; **verified with the gate exact invocation** (bare `node <jestBin>` `--ci --silent`): **7/7** | The spec spawns a Node child to exercise the real `@auth/core`. Under `pnpm exec jest` it passed — twice alone, and again across `src/shared/quality` (49 suites, 2292 tests). `scripts/test-ratchet.js` spawns jest as a bare `node <jestBin>`, the child inherited no `NODE_PATH`, and it died on `Cannot find module @auth/core/errors`. **Root cause: the anchor was a SYMLINK.** Under pnpm `apps/web/node_modules/next-auth` links into `node_modules/.pnpm/next-auth@…/node_modules/`, where `@auth/core` is a SIBLING; it exists nowhere under `apps/web/node_modules` and nowhere in the root `node_modules`. `createRequire` resolves from the path it is handed, so the symlink anchor walked upward and found nothing. Fixed with `realpathSync`, which is also what Node itself does at runtime. **THE EXPENSIVE PART WAS THE MIS-DIAGNOSIS, and it is the lesson.** The first hypothesis was TIMEOUTS, and every fact supporting it was true — no `testTimeout` in `apps/api/jest.config.js` (so jest default 5000 ms), a **bare `node -e 1` spawn measured 1216 ms** under full-suite load, three spawns per file, and the failure reproduced byte-identically twice (`4369/4376 · 7 failing · 4 known-failing`). It cost a full ~15-minute gate cycle and the "fix" returned **four** failures instead of three. **A plausible mechanism that predicts the symptom is not a diagnosis**; the actual error text was three minutes away (`--ci --silent` on the one file, via bare node). Two rules worth carrying: **a green `pnpm exec jest` is evidence about `pnpm exec`, not about the gate** — reproduce with the gate own invocation before believing anything — and **read the error before modelling the cause.** **The general remedy is NOT implemented and has no owner:** ten other specs under `apps/api/src/shared/quality/` call `execFileSync`/`spawnSync` (`boot-gate`, `link-integrity-gate`, `schema-drift-gate`, `production-artefact-gate`, `csv-escape-gate`, `audit-write-gate`, `audit-provenance-gate`, `postgres-client-path-gate`, `test-ratchet`), none audited here for inherited resolution — though a grep confirms NONE of them uses `createRequire`, so the symlink-anchor form of the bug is unique to this file | G-DNC |
| PF-448 **P2 `[tooling]`** *(raised by the LAND PASS of run 96 / `S-E05-8`; the sprint named the instance itself, this row names the CLASS)* — **six shipped artefacts cited a discharge mechanism that did not exist; "shipped NOT EXECUTED" and "not shipped at all" are different facts** | V3-E05 *(gate integrity)* | **S-E05-8** *(instance fixed at land: STEP 6 written)* | `open` — **the instance is fixed; the CLASS is recorded** | none — a citation to a script step has no assertable surface today, which is precisely the gap | `direct-grant-failure.ts`, `PortalLoginForm.tsx`, `ADR-082 §D3`, `stories/S-E05-8.md`, `v3-e05/PROGRESS.md` and `PF-444`'s own row each cited `scripts/keycloak-live-probe.js` **"STEP 6 — mint C"** as the observation that would settle premise P-1. That script was **unmodified and stopped at STEP 5**, so the pre-decided contingency had no trigger and `PF-444` would have aged into folklore behind a citation to nothing. The sprint **found this itself** and said so, which is the only reason it was cheap to fix. Same family as `PF-374` / `PF-388` / `PF-442` (a cited line number and a cited test verdict are CLAIMS, never measurements) with the pointer running the other way: those cite a *result* that moved, this cites a *mechanism* that was never built. **A cheap detector exists and is not written:** a gate that extracts `scripts/<file>.js` + `STEP <n>` citations from docs and source and asserts the step string occurs in the named file | G-DNC |
| PF-450 **P3 `[tooling]`** *(raised by run 97 / the autonomy land pass — DIAGNOSED and deliberately NOT fixed, per RULE 0 clause 6 "record, don't fix")* — **a gate assertion is pinned to WALL-CLOCK time, so it fails under the load of the very suite that runs it** | V3-E05 *(gate integrity)* | — *(no story; recorded only)* | `open` — the instance is transient, the CLASS is real: **a timing assertion inside a 4400-test suite measures the machine, not the code** | none — the flake is in the assertion, not in a product path | `apps/api/src/shared/quality/schema-drift-gate.spec.ts:1218` (`probeServer takes the same fast path and its contract is unchanged (AC-8)`) asserts `Date.now() - startedAt` is `< 5000`. It **FAILED** in the first `ci-gate.sh` pass of run 97 and **PASSED** in the second, on a tree whose only difference was an unrelated docs sync — and it **passes in isolation in 7.7 s** under the ratchet's own bare-`node` invocation. The full `test:api` ratchet runs ~4400 tests across ~20 node workers and saturates the machine, so a 5-second wall-clock ceiling is a coin flip under load. **The premise is sound but under-specified:** `probeServer` genuinely should be fast, and `DEAD_URL` targets port `59999` which is closed — verified with `netstat`, so this is NOT the `AC-P16` container-up family (`TOOL-36`), where the cause was `pilotage_postgres` listening. It is purely load. **Two cautions for whoever fixes it.** (1) The obvious fix — raise the ceiling — weakens a gate to reach green, which Step −2 forbids; the honest fix asserts the *fast path was taken* (a code-shape or call-count assertion) rather than how many milliseconds it took. (2) The named-test evidence here is unusually strong and should not be re-derived: FAIL then PASS across two full gate cycles, plus a green isolated run using the gate's exact invocation — so `PF-449`'s rule is satisfied, this is not a `pnpm exec` artefact. Recording rather than fixing is the deliberate choice: it costs a ratchet re-run occasionally, and a run spent on it is a run the roadmap does not get | G-DNC |

| PF-420 **P2** *(raised by run 94 / `S-E03-9`; RECORD, DON'T FIX — RULE 0 clause 6)* — **the parent dashboard issues 2N round trips of a heavy aggregate, from the client** | V3-E03 | — *(story to be written; the remedy is a new `/analytics/parent-dashboard/batch` aggregate, so it is a contract slice, not an edit)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-27 in source, deliberately NOT fixed** | none — recorded, not fixed | `apps/web/src/app/parent/dashboard/page.tsx:277-295`. This is audit A2's *"parent dashboard fans out per child"* clause, and it is **already bounded and already parallelised**: `FAMILY_OVERVIEW_MAX = 8` (`:62`) caps the family-overview swimlane, and the fetches run under `Promise.all`. What remains is that it is still **2N round trips** — `/analytics/parent-dashboard/:id` **and** `/alerts/parent/:id` per child — of the platform's heaviest aggregate, which is GUARDRAILS §2's *"never N+1 from the client"* in the one place the north star names as the core (parent dashboard, five questions, **under 2 s**). **The structural reason it is not fixed inside `S-E03-9`:** collapsing it means a new route, a new response contract and a new permission surface, which is a slice of its own; `S-E03-9` is a pagination-and-truth slice and widening it would be the "finish the feature" that GUARDRAILS §5 forbids | G-TRUTH |
| PF-424 **P3** *(raised by run 94 / `S-E03-9`; RECORD, DON'T FIX)* — **`AssignmentsManager`'s four filters run CLIENT-side over the fetched array, while the endpoint already accepts the same four as query params** | V3-E03 | — *(story to be written; it is a UX/state slice — loading states and URL sync — with no truth content)* | `open` — **created by design as a consequence of `S-E03-9`'s `AC-4`, and disclosed rather than hidden** | none — recorded, not fixed | `apps/web/src/app/admin/teaching-assignments/AssignmentsManager.tsx` (the teacher / class / subject / year `<select>`s) against `apps/api/src/modules/teaching/teaching-assignments.controller.ts:57-61`, which has accepted `teacherProfileId`, `classSectionId`, `subjectId` and `academicYearId` **since before this slice**. Once `AC-4` bounds the endpoint, **a client-side filter over a page is a lie**: the user narrows to one teacher and sees only that teacher's rows *within the window*, with no signal that others exist beyond it. `S-E03-9` mitigates rather than fixes — `AC-5c` requires a truncation notice in the shape `parent/dashboard/page.tsx:486-489` already uses whenever `data.length < total`. The real fix is to bind the four `<select>`s to the four query params that are already there | G-TRUTH |

Two of the remaining three are unbounded list endpoints, and they are **not incidental**: they are the *inputs* to the coverage aggregate (`AC-5b` differences the whole-set `classes` and `subjects` props against server-derived id sets), so bounding them would re-open `PF-421` from the other side. They must be bounded **together with** whatever replaces the coverage differencing — which is why this is a row and not a follow-up commit | G-TRUTH |
| PF-427 **P2** *(raised by the LAND PASS of run 94 / `S-E03-9` — this slice PRODUCED the defect, and a human reading two files caught it, not a test)* — **`api<T>()` is an unvalidated cast, so a server payload and the page that reads it can disagree silently while BOTH sides typecheck green** | V3-E03 | **`S-E03-11`** *(run 95 — **advanced, NOT closed**)* | `in-progress` — **ADVANCED by `S-E03-11`, explicitly NOT closed** | `apps/api/src/shared/pagination/page-envelope.spec.ts` (26 cases — the red-before/green-after of the factory, including run 94’s exact payload rejected by name) · `apps/api/src/shared/quality/page-envelope-boundary-gate.spec.ts` (the one-way ratchet, `R1_CEILING = 1`, `R2_CEILING = 6`, allowlist EMPTY, two fixtures proving the classifier bites) | **Not hypothetical — it happened inside `S-E03-9` and came within one review of shipping.** The slice's two halves were written in two checkouts; the API emitted `totals`, the admin page read `summary`. Each half typechecked GREEN *against its own hand-written shape*, because `api<T>()` **asserts** `T` rather than verifying it. On the merged tree that renders the four `/admin/assignments` KPIs as `—` and the coverage panel as permanently unavailable — i.e. this slice would have shipped a truth defect WORSE than the one `PF-50` names, in the very act of closing it. The general shape: every `apps/web` read of a server aggregate is a **second, hand-maintained copy of a contract that already exists** — `project_paired_lists_drift` at the HTTP boundary, and the same failure mode `ADR-080` removes for *query* shapes. Candidate remedy: derive the page's response type from the same `packages/contracts` module the controller returns, so the two cannot be spelled differently. **ADVANCED by `S-E03-11` (run 95, `ADR-081`) and NOT closed.** Two clauses of this row were TRUE when written and are now FALSE, corrected rather than left to mislead the next reader: there IS a story (`S-E03-11`), and the response-shape test WAS written (`apps/api/src/shared/pagination/page-envelope.spec.ts`, 26 cases). What the slice did: `pageEnvelope()` in `packages/contracts/src/pagination/page-envelope.ts` declares the frame ONCE; two API emitters DECLARE their return shape so renaming `totals`→`summary` **in `apps/api`** stops compiling; four admin readers PARSE it through `apiEnvelope()` and raise a `ResponseShapeError` that NAMES the offending key. **The census is ELEVEN reads over SEVEN files, not the four the brief asserted** — the sprint re-measured and corrected its own brief, and the correction is load-bearing: a ratchet frozen at four would have legalised seven live occurrences. **Why it is NOT closed, stated rather than discovered later:** `data` and `total` are now genuinely common, but the EXTENSION keys (`totals`/`coverage`/`limit`/`offset` on assignments, `kpis`/`filters` on audit) remain **two hand-written lists in two packages** — which is exactly where run 94’s defect lived. The gain is real (a silent `undefined` becomes a named runtime error) but it is a RUNTIME guard, not the compile-time closure the client-side docblocks imply. Residual class: `PF-428` (6 inline casts), `PF-429` (parent portal), `PF-431` (88 bare-`{data}` casts), `PF-441` (the API-side twin). Do NOT close this by adding one assertion to one page | G-TRUTH |

| TOOL-26 **P1** *(discovered by run 51, raised by the slice that caused most of it)* — enabling the drift gate's end-to-end block turned `pnpm test` into a DDL-executing operation, and four smaller residuals came with it | V3-E02 *(gate power)* | — *(story to be written; deliberately **not** batched into `TOOL-25`, whose PR is already a full slice)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-13 (run 51)**, partly by the implementing sprint's own verify panel and partly by the routine at land | `apps/api/src/shared/quality/schema-drift-gate.spec.ts` §5f (the loopback guard) and its six injected cases | **Five residuals, in descending order of how much they matter.** **(1) `pnpm test` is now a DDL-executing operation, and the bound is `loopback`, not `disposable`.** The block really runs `CREATE DATABASE` / `prisma migrate deploy` / `DROP DATABASE … WITH (FORCE)` against whatever PostgreSQL answers on the resolved address. `TOOL-25` **tightened** this (previously the bound was the `schema_drift_%` **name** alone; it is now name **and** loopback, classified on the TCP preflight before any credentialed probe), so the direction of travel is right — but **loopback ≠ non-production**: `.env.prod.example:50` binds a deployment to the loopback interface, so the predicate that makes this safe *here* would not make it safe *there*. On this host it is exactly what Step −1 authorises (local data is expendable, there is no production), which is why `TOOL-25` merged. What is owed is an **`ADR-027` addendum** stating the limitation honestly, or its own slice. **Do not "fix" this by re-disabling the block** — that restores the false green `TOOL-25` closed. **(2) The port half of the cross-server guard lost its only coverage.** Every remaining case now pairs *different-host* addresses, so `schema-drift-check.js:1312`'s port clause is unasserted: a regression that compared only hosts would go unnoticed. Cheapest of the five, and squarely the `TOOL-21` shape (an assertion whose meaning quietly evaporated). **(3) `leaves no scratch database behind (AC-11)` asserts a SERVER-WIDE property.** It checks that the `schema_drift_%` list is empty across the whole server and now really executes, so two overlapping runs flake it — and the routine's own `MAX_INFLIGHT=2` makes overlap reachable. Verified `(none)` this run, so it is correct today and structurally fragile tomorrow. **(4) Two assertions print the raw resolved DSN, password included, on failure** (`schema-drift-gate.spec.ts:1720-1721` assert on `RESOLVED_BASE_URL` directly, and jest prints the *received* value) — into a tail this routine pastes into PR bodies. Confirmed by reading the sites rather than by relaying the claim. The credential is the well-known local `pilotage:pilotage` that already appears throughout these ledgers, so this is **hygiene, not a leak** — but it is the kind that stops being hygiene the day the address is not local. **(5) In a linked worktree `.env` is absent**, so `defaultDatabaseUrl()` falls back to the historic literal and the five cases skip again. **The closing measurement is only meaningful from the main checkout** — recorded because a future run that measures from a worktree will read a green skip and believe it. **Ledger note, and it is the reason this row exists at all:** `TOOL-25` is the second consecutive finding whose true cause was a **comment asserting a constraint nobody re-measured** (`TOOL-22`'s port, run 50's `psql` host capability, now A6's reach). The transferable rule is run 50's, sharpened: **a comment that explains why a value must be wrong is a claim, and a claim that has survived three slices is overdue for one command.** | G-MIGRATION, G-DNC
| TOOL-27 **P1** *(discovered by run 53, by the routine reading its own sprint's failure list)* — the sprint returns `landed: true` unchanged when its VERIFICATION agents die, so a slice can ship with its adversarial review, security panel and test panel all absent and nothing in the verdict says so | V3-E02 *(gate power)* | — *(recorded with its measurement; **not** storified this run — deliberately not batched into `S-E01-2c`, whose PR is already a full security slice, and fixing the harness inside the run whose evidence it undermines is the wrong order)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-13 (run 53)** from the workflow's own `<failures>` block and its `<usage>` counters, not inferred | `bmad/workflows/sprint.workflow.js` — the return value is assembled without reference to which agents failed | **Three of seventeen agents died on `API Error: 529 Overloaded`: `quinn:adversarial`, `panel:security`, `panel:test`. The workflow still returned `landed: true`, and `agents_error: 3` appears only in the harness's usage block — never in the verdict the routine reads to decide AUTO-LAND.** The three that failed were **exactly** the three whose job is to disbelieve the other fourteen, on a slice the sprint had itself risk-tiered `[schema][security][auth]` with the words *"expects the escalation panel and the human-review flag"*. So the run that most needed adversarial review is the run that silently did without it. **Why this is P1 and not hygiene:** `S-E01-2b` (run 52) had its panel inject **5 mutants into the migration and kill 5** — that is the instrument which distinguishes *"the policies pass a test I wrote"* from *"the policies survive someone trying to defeat them"*, and `S-E01-2c` shipped 5 new authorization policies with **nobody having attempted to defeat them**. The routine substituted a hand read of the migration and the check, which is real but is **not** mutation testing, and the PR says so rather than implying equivalence. **The defect is not the 529** — a transient upstream error is not preventable here. The defect is that a **partial** panel is reported as a **complete** one. Fix direction: the sprint's return value must carry the failed-agent set, and `landed` must degrade (or the PR body must be forced to carry a `⚠ VERIFICATION INCOMPLETE` block naming the missing agents) when any *verification* agent — as opposed to an implementation agent — did not complete. A retry would also help, but retry is the smaller half: **the load-bearing change is that the verdict stops overstating what produced it.** That is the same sentence as `TOOL-24` (*"a verdict must name the instrument that produced it"*) and the same one as `TOOL-13` (*"the ratchet must not certify a check it did not perform"*) — this is the third address of one rule, now at the harness layer rather than inside a gate script. **RECURRED IN RUN 55, as predicted, and this is the second consecutive instance:** `john:spec` died on `API Error: 529 Overloaded` and the sprint again returned `landed: true` — this time on a `[schema][security]` slice that adds a column, a foreign key, an RLS policy and a GRANT. **The agent that died was the one that writes the story spec**, so the slice shipped with its acceptance criteria never authored *before* the code. The routine wrote `docs/spec/features/v3-e01/stories/S-E01-2d.md` afterwards and **labelled it, in its own §0, as reconstructed post-hoc and therefore not a constraint on the implementation** — which is the honest disposition, not a repair. Two runs in a row now, the verdict has been earned by fewer agents than it claims: run 53 lost the three adversarial/panel agents, run 55 lost the spec agent. **The pattern is no longer "an agent occasionally dies"; it is "the verdict is insensitive to WHICH agent died", and losing the spec agent is qualitatively different from losing a builder** because a spec written after the fact cannot fail the code | G-DNC
| PF-183 **P1** *(discovered by run 52, by the slice that installed the policies)* — six tenant-DERIVED tables carry no `tenant_id`, so they sit outside every `tenant_isolation` policy, and the connection cutover will meet them before anyone else does | V3-E01 | **`S-E01-2c`** *(run 53 — storified and DECIDED as `ADR-042`; kept out of `S-E01-2b` deliberately, and settled BEFORE the `S-E01-1` cutover exactly as this row required)* | `in-progress` — **the decision is recorded (`ADR-042`, 2026-08-13); the EXECUTED proof is what closes the row, and it has not run yet.** Deliberately not marked closed at planning time: calling a policy proven before it has denied anything is the `PF-02` defect one level down · originally **measured 2026-08-13 (run 52)** against the live catalog, then re-derived from the migration's own grant list rather than relayed from the sprint | `scripts/rls-isolation-check.js` — its `TENANT_COLS` count is catalog-derived, which is what makes this gap *visible* rather than assumed | **The 44 policied tables are exactly the tables that carry a `tenant_id` column. Six more belong to a tenant by FOREIGN KEY and therefore receive nothing:** `grade_revision`, `announcement_receipt`, `branding`, `import_row`, `user_role`, `outbox_event`. (`tenant` itself and `_prisma_migrations` are correctly excluded — `tenant`'s primary key *is* the discriminant, and the ledger is not tenant data. `permission`, `role` and `role_permission` are reference data by design.) **Today this is fail-closed, not a leak, and the distinction is the whole point of the row:** the migration GRANTs to `app_user` only the 44 it policies, so after the cutover `app_user` would receive `permission denied` on all six rather than an unfiltered read. **The hazard is at the cutover, and it forks two ways, both bad:** grant them without a policy and `user_role` — the RBAC assignment table — becomes a cross-tenant read for the application role; leave them ungranted and every feature touching revisions, receipts, branding, imports, role assignment or the outbox breaks with a permission error that will read like a bug in the feature. **Neither branch is acceptable, so the six need a decision before `S-E01-1` runs, not during it.** Fix direction: a policy predicated on the FK path (`EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM parent p WHERE p.id = child.parent_id AND p.tenant_id = nullif(current_setting(…),'')::uuid)`), which needs an index on each FK before it is enabled — the same R-11 discipline `S-E01-2b` discharged for the 44 — **or** a deliberate, recorded denormalisation adding `tenant_id` to each. Do not resolve it by widening the grant. **RULING, `S-E01-2c` / `ADR-042` (2026-08-13) — and it corrects this row's own premise in three places, each re-measured against `pg_constraint` on the live catalog rather than relayed.** **(a) FIVE derive by FK, not six.** `outbox_event` holds **no foreign key at all** — polymorphic `aggregate_type` + `aggregate_id`, unconstrained — so there is no path to predicate on and none may be invented. The `S-E01-2b` migration header (line 156, *« AUCUNE FK, aucun discriminant de tenant »*) was already right; `NEXT.md`'s *"all six derive their tenant by FOREIGN KEY"* overstated it. `outbox_event` is deferred **by name**, stays fail-closed, and both its absent grant and its absent policy are **asserted** so a later run cannot widen the grant to silence the permission error — see **`PF-185`**. **(b) "every feature … breaks" is true of five, not six.** Measured: `outboxEvent.` has **zero** callers in `apps/**` or `packages/**`. The table is scaffolding with no writer, so its fail-closed cost at the cutover is **zero features broken** — which is what makes deferring it safe rather than merely convenient. **(c) No index needs to be created.** All five FK columns already **lead** an existing index (`grade_revision_grade_id_revised_at_idx`; `announcement_receipt_announcement_id_user_profile_id_key`; `branding_pkey` — the column *is* the PK; `import_row_batch_id_row_index_key`; `user_role_user_profile_id_role_id_school_id_key`), and every parent side is a PK probe. R-11's precondition is **already discharged**; the work is to **assert** it, not to add it. **Two decisions this row did not anticipate, both load-bearing.** **(i) The census formula the finding implies is vacuous.** `RLS_ON == TENANT_COLS + DERIVED_POLICIED`, where the derived half counts tables that *have* a policy, passes on the exact case it exists to catch: ship a sixth derived table with no policy and `DERIVED_POLICIED` stays 5, `RLS_ON` stays 49, `49 == 44 + 5` → green. The agreement must be against `DERIVED_EXPECTED`, computed from `pg_constraint` (base tables with no `tenant_id` holding an FK to one), so the sixth raises the expectation to 6 while `RLS_ON` stays 49 and the gate **fails** (`ADR-042 §D3`). **(ii) `ON DELETE CASCADE` defeats append-only, and saying otherwise would repeat `PF-02` one level down.** All five FKs are `CASCADE`; PostgreSQL runs RI actions as the referencing table's owner and does not apply RLS to them, so `app_user` — holding `DELETE` on `grade` — removes `grade_revision` rows via the parent despite the child being `SELECT, INSERT` only. The cascade **cannot cross a tenant** (the parent must be visible first), so it is tenant-safe and audit-unsafe; the audit half is `PF-186`, not fixed here | G-TENANT, G-DNC |
| PF-184 **P2** *(discovered by run 52)* — an out-of-band `DROP POLICY` on a running database is invisible to every gate this repository has | V3-E02 *(gate power)* | — *(recorded, **not** storified — routine triage)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-13 (run 52)**; the narrower half of the claim was verified by reading the check rather than trusting the sprint's summary | `scripts/rls-isolation-check.js` (what closes the CI half) vs `scripts/schema-drift-check.js` (what cannot) | **`prisma migrate diff` cannot see policies.** With `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`, `CREATE POLICY tenant_isolation` and the `app_user` GRANTs installed, the drift diff returns *"No difference detected"*, exit 0, byte-identical to the clean baseline — confirmed this run inside `ci-gate.sh`, where `SCHEMA DRIFT CHECK: PASS — 3 migration(s) built 55 tables and the datamodel adds nothing` printed on a tree whose newest migration is 447 lines of policy DDL. That is *good* news for G-MIGRATION (no false red) and it is the load-bearing bad news. **The half that IS covered, and the sprint's framing overstated the gap here — corrected by reading the code:** `rls-isolation-check.js` derives its `TENANT_COLS` count from `information_schema.columns` on the live catalog, not from a frozen list, so a **45th** `tenant_id` table shipped without a policy makes `TENANT_COLS` and `RLS_ON` disagree and **fails**. Since adding a table necessarily touches `apps/api/prisma/`, the gate's trigger predicate fires. That path is closed. **The half that is genuinely open:** nothing observes a *running* database. A `DROP POLICY` / `ALTER TABLE … DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` executed by hand against a live instance leaves CI green forever, because every check above runs against a **scratch** database built from the ledger, never against the instance serving traffic. This is a monitoring gap, not a CI gap, and it should be fixed as one — a startup assertion or a health probe that counts policies against `tenant_id` columns and refuses to serve on a mismatch. It becomes load-bearing the moment `S-E01-1` performs the cutover, because from then on a dropped policy is a silent cross-tenant read rather than a harmless one | G-MIGRATION, G-TENANT, G-DNC |

Decided in **`ADR-044`**, which discharges `ADR-042 §D7` · originally **measured 2026-08-13 (run 53)** against `pg_constraint` on the live catalog and a grep of `apps/**` + `packages/**` for callers | `scripts/rls-isolation-check.js` — the six `S-E01-2c` assertions **INVERTED, not deleted** (policy `0 → 1`, `relrowsecurity` `false → true`, grants `none → SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE`, `SELECT` refused → `SELECT` accepted **and returning only own-tenant rows**), plus four with no predecessor: the `NOT NULL` column, the cascade FK, the leading index, and `OUTBOX_DELETE = 0`. It is also the one table of the eleven that carries neither a `tenant_id` column nor a derivable path, which is why `ADR-042` decides five and defers exactly one. **Correcting `PF-183` and `NEXT.md`, which both said all six derive by FK: five do.** The `S-E01-2b` migration header (line 156) was already accurate. **Why deferring is safe rather than convenient, and this is the load-bearing measurement:** `outboxEvent.` has **zero** callers anywhere in `apps/**` or `packages/**` — the table is scaffolding with no writer — so leaving it ungranted through the `S-E01-1` cutover breaks **nothing**. It is a genuinely empty fail-closed cost, not an accepted one. **What it must not become:** the day a writer appears, the tempting fix is a `GRANT` to silence the `permission denied`. That branch hands the application role an **unfiltered, cross-tenant event log** — every aggregate of every tenant. `ADR-042 §D7` therefore asserts *both* absences by name so the widening cannot happen quietly. **Fix, when it is storified:** add `tenant_id uuid` to `OutboxEvent` in `schema.prisma`, backfill existing rows *(currently zero, so the backfill is a formality today and stops being one the moment a writer lands — another reason to do it early)*, make it `NOT NULL` in a second migration, then give it the ordinary `tenant_isolation` policy of the 44 and drop it out of the derived set entirely. | PF-186 **P2** *(declared by run 53 / `S-E01-2c` / `ADR-042 §D6`, as the honest limit of that slice's own append-only claim)* — `ON DELETE CASCADE` is subject to neither RLS nor the child's grant, so `grade_revision` is append-only against direct DML but **not** against a parent cascade | V3-E01 *(audit integrity)* | — *(recorded, **not** storified — routine triage; it is a `schema.prisma` **and** behaviour change, not a policy change)* | `open` — **measured 2026-08-13 (run 53)**: `pg_constraint.confdeltype = 'c'` on all five derived FKs, cross-checked against PostgreSQL's documented rule that referential-integrity actions run as the referencing table's owner and are not filtered by row-level security | `scripts/rls-isolation-check.js` — the privilege-shape assertion (`grade_revision` ⊆ {SELECT, INSERT}) proves the **direct** half only, and the migration says so | **`grade_revision` is the grade audit trail (G-AUDIT), and `ADR-042 §D5` grants it `SELECT, INSERT` only** — the same reasoning `ADR-032 §D7` applies to `audit_log`: a correction of a correction is a new row, never an edit. **That claim is true of direct DML and false of a cascade.** All five derived FKs are `ON DELETE CASCADE`; PostgreSQL executes RI actions as the owner of the referencing table and does **not** apply RLS to them, so `app_user` — which holds `DELETE` on `grade`, `announcement`, `school`, `import_batch` and `user_profile` — removes `grade_revision` rows by deleting the parent grade, despite the child's grant. **Writing "the grade audit trail is unrewritable" without this sentence would be the `PF-02` over-claim repeated one level down, which is precisely the defect `V3-E01` exists to end** — so it is recorded rather than smoothed over. **What the cascade cannot do:** cross a tenant. Deleting the parent requires *seeing* it, and the parent's own `tenant_isolation` policy confines that to the current tenant. The cascade is therefore **tenant-safe and audit-unsafe**, and only the second half is open. **Fix direction:** `ON DELETE RESTRICT` on `grade_revision.grade_id` plus a soft-delete for `Grade`, so a graded row is retired rather than erased. That is a schema change **and** a behaviour change (every grade-delete call site must learn the new verb), which is why it is not batched into a policy slice. Lower priority than `PF-185` because it needs an authenticated actor who already holds `grades.delete` in their own tenant — a destructive insider action, not a boundary crossing | G-AUDIT, G-TENANT |
